Figure 21.
What our decomposition would provide

{exa}

(Continuation of Example
3.1.1
) We thus have
!_{f,1}[\mathcal{N}_{1}(f)]

=\{o_{2},o_{4}\}
; and indeed
o_{2}
and
o_{4}
are the boxes of depth
1
at depth
0
of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[2](e)\equiv R
(see Figure
11
). Moreover we have
\mathcal{K}_{10,1}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[1](e))=\{p_{1},p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{2}\}
and
\mathcal{K}_{10,2}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[1](e))=\{p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{4}\}
; and indeed, in Figure
11
, we have
t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[2](e)}[\{o_{2}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[2](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o_{2})]=

\{p_{1},p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{2}\}
and
t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[2](e)}[\{o_{4}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[2](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o_{4})]=

\{p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{4}\}
.

{defi}

Let
R
be an in-PS. For any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we define a subset
\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p)
of
\mathcal{B}^{\geq i}(R)
: we set
\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p)=\{b_{R}^{\geq i}(q);(q\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R)\wedge t_{R}(q)=p)\}
.

A crucial lemma is the following one:

Lemma 29
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
. Then, for any
j>0
, we have
p\in\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
if, and only if, there exists
o\in\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p)
such that
k^{j}\in e^{\#}(o)
. Moreover we have
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(p)\textit{ mod }k={\textit{a}}_{R^{\leq i}}(p)
.

Proof 3.7
.

By Proposition
21
, we have

{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(p)={\textit{a}}_{R^{\leq i}}(p)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R)\\
t_{R}(p^{\prime})=p\end{subarray}}\sum e^{\#}(b_{R}^{\geq i}(p^{\prime}))

Moreover we have

•

{\textit{a}}_{R^{\leq i}}(p)<k
;

•

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R);t_{R}(p^{\prime})=p\}\right)<k
;

•

and
(\forall p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R))(\forall m\in e^{\#}(b_{R}^{\geq i}(p^{\prime})))(\exists j>0)k^{j}\in e^{\#}(b_{R}^{\leq i}(p^{\prime}))
.

Hence

•

{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(p)\textit{ mod }k={\textit{a}}_{R^{\leq i}}(p)

•

and
p\in\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
if, and only if,
(\exists p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R))(t_{R}(p^{\prime})=p\wedge k^{j}\in e^{\#}(b_{R}^{\geq i}(p^{\prime})))
, i.e.
(\exists o\in\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p))k^{j}\in e^{\#}(o)
.

Fact 30
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
e
be a pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
. Let
q\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R)
. Then we have
b_{R}^{\geq i}(q)=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)
if, and only if, there exists
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o))
such that
q=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o,q^{\prime})
.

Proof 3.8
.

By induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
. We assume that
b_{R}^{\geq i}(q)=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)
and we distinguish between two cases:

•

o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
: we have
\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)=o
, hence there exists
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\leq i}(B_{R}(o))
such that
q=(o,q^{\prime})
; but
B_{R}(o)=B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o)
;

•

o=(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime}))
for some
o_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
,
e_{1}\in e(o_{1})
and
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[e_{1}](i))
: we have
\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)=(o_{1},\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o^{\prime}))
, hence there exists
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(B_{R}(o_{1}))
such that
q=(o_{1},q_{0})
and
b_{B_{R}(o_{1})}^{\geq i}(q_{0})=\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o^{\prime})
; by induction hypothesis, there exists
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})}(o^{\prime}))
such that
q_{0}=\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o_{1},q^{\prime})
; but
B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})}(o^{\prime})=B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o)
.

Conversely, we assume that there exists
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o))
such that
q=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o,q^{\prime})
and we distinguish between two cases:

•

o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
: we have
b_{R}^{\geq i}(\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o,q^{\prime}))=b_{R}^{\geq i}(o,q^{\prime})=o=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)
;

•

o=(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime}))
for some
o_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
,
e_{1}\in e(o_{1})
and
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[e_{1}](i))
: we have
b_{R}^{\geq i}(\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o,q^{\prime}))=b_{R}^{\geq i}(o_{1},(\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o^{\prime},q^{\prime})))=(o_{1},b_{B_{R}(o_{1})}^{\geq i}(\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o^{\prime},q^{\prime})))
; since
B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o)=B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})}(o^{\prime})
, we can apply the induction hypothesis and we thus obtain
b_{R}^{\geq i}(\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o,q^{\prime}))=(o_{1},\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})(o^{\prime}))=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(o)
.

Proposition 31
.

Let
R
be an in-PS.
Let
k>\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R){}\right),\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
and let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we have
\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))=!_{e,i}[\mathcal{N}_{i,e}]
. Moreover, the set
\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
is empty. Furthermore, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]
and, if
\oc_{e,i}(j)\notin\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then there exist
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
and
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)=\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
.
In particular, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
.

Proof 3.9
.

It is trivial to check, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, that we have

(\forall o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))(\forall p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(o)))\kappa_{R}[i](e)(o,p)\notin\mathcal{B}^{=i}(R)\>\>\>(\ast)

Now, we prove, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, that
\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))=!_{e,i}[\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)]
:

•

Let
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
. By Lemma
28
, there exists
o\in\mathcal{B}^{=i}(R)
such that
j\in\log_{k}[e^{\#}(o)]
, hence
k^{j}\in e^{\#}(o)
. Since
k^{j}\in e^{\#}((\kappa_{R}[i](e)\circ\oc_{e,i})(j))
, we have
(\kappa_{R}[i](e)\circ\oc_{e,i})(j)=o\in\mathcal{B}^{=i}(R)
. If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then
\oc_{e,i}(j)=o
. Otherwise, there exist
o_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
and
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}^{=i}(B_{R}(o_{1}))
such that
o=(o_{1},o^{\prime})
: By
(\ast)
, there exist
e_{1}\in e(o_{1})
and
o^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i](e_{1}))
such that
\kappa_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i](e_{1})(o^{\prime\prime})=o^{\prime}
and
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime\prime}))
, hence
o^{\prime\prime}=\oc_{e_{1},i}(j)
. By induction hypothesis, we have
\oc_{e_{1},i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[e_{1}](i+1))
, hence
(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime\prime}))\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[e](i+1))
.

•

Conversely, let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
. Let
j\in\log_{k}[e^{\#}(o)]
. By Lemma
28
, we have
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
. We have
k^{j}\in e^{\#}(o)
and
k^{j}\in e^{\#}((\kappa_{R}[i](e)\circ\oc_{e,i})(j))
, hence
(\kappa_{R}[i](e)\circ\oc_{e,i})(j)=o
. If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then
\oc_{e,i}(j)=o
. Otherwise, there exist
o_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
,
e_{1}\in e(o_{1})
and
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o_{1})}[i+1](e_{1})})
such that
o=(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime}))
. By induction hypothesis, there exists
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{1})
such that
\oc_{e_{1},i}(j)=o^{\prime}
: we have
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o_{1},(e_{1},o^{\prime}))
.

By Lemma
28
and Lemma
29
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)=\emptyset
. Moreover, for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
, for any
e_{o}\in e(o)
, again by Lemma
28
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)
, hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap(\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})))

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\emptyset)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\emptyset\textit{ (by induction hypothesis)}

We showed
\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathbb{N}\setminus(\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)\cup\{0\})}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))=\emptyset

(\ast\ast)
.

Now, let
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
. We distinguish between two cases:

•

\oc_{e,i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
: By Lemma
29
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)=\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R);\oc_{e,i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p)\}=t_{R}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]
. Moreover, by
(\ast\ast)
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
. We thus have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]
.

•

\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
for some
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
: By induction hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))=t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}[\{\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))]
. By
(\ast\ast)
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)\cup\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
, hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}[\{\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R);(\exists o\in\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p))k^{j}\in e^{\#}(o)\}

(by Lemma
29
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R);\kappa_{R}[i](e)(\oc_{e,i}(j))\in\mathcal{B}_{R}^{\geq i}(p)\}

(by Lemma
26
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R);(\exists q\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R))(b_{R}^{\geq i}(q)=\kappa_{R}[i](e)(\oc_{e,i}(j))\wedge t_{R}(q)=p)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R);(\exists q\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R))(b_{R}^{\geq i}(q)=\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(\oc_{e,i}(j))\wedge t_{R}(q)=p)\}

(by Lemma
23
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{t_{R}(\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(\oc_{e,i}(j),q^{\prime}));q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\}

(by Fact
30
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{t_{R}(\kappa_{R}[i+1](e)(\oc_{e,i}(j),q^{\prime}));q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\}

(by Fact
17
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)

As the following example shows, the information we obtain is already non-trivial, but far away to be strong enough.

{exa}

The PS’s
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
,
R_{3}
and
R_{4}
of Figure
26
, Figure
26
, Figure
26
and Figure
26
respectively have the same LPS, which is depicted in Figure
22
. But if we know that
p\in t_{R}[\{o_{1}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o_{1})]\cap t_{R}[\{o_{2}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o_{2})]
, then we know that
R\not=R_{3}
and
R\not=R_{4}
: The information we already obtain is an information that is not obtained from
LPS(R)
. Still we are not yet able to distinguish between
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
. Since
LPS(R_{1})=LPS(R_{2})
and
\textit{depth}(R_{1})=1=\textit{depth}(R_{2})
, the sets of pseudo-experiments on
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
coincide. Now, for any pseudo-experiment on these two proof-nets
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
, we have
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{1}}[0](e)}(o_{1})={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{2}}[0](e)}(o_{1})
,
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{1}}[0](e)}(o_{2})={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{2}}[0](e)}(o_{2})
and
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{1}}[0](e)}(p)={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{2}}[0](e)}(p)
, which shows that the arity of exponential ports in the Taylor expansion is not sufficient to recover the PS.

Corollary 32
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
. Let
\varphi
be some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
. Let
R_{o}
be an in-PS such that
R_{o}=\varphi\cdot_{o}R
. Let
k>\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R){}\right),\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
, let
e_{o}\in e(o)
, let
i\in\mathbb{N}
and let
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
. Then
\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o}))\cap\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\subseteq\varphi[\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\cap\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))]
.

Proof 3.10
.

Notice first that, since the set
\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
is non-empty, by Proposition
31
, we have
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
. By Proposition
31
again, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}[\{\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))]\cap\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\varphi[\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\cap t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]]

Now, let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))
such that
t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)\in\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
18
, we have
p\notin\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))
and
t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)=\varphi(t_{R}(o,\kappa_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)))
. Moreover, since
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
, we obtain
(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o}))
, hence, by Fact
17
,
\kappa_{B_{R}(o)}[e_{o}](i+1)(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(o))
: We thus showed
\kappa_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
, which entails
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))
and
t_{R}(o,\kappa_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j),p))=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j),p)
.

3.2.
Connected components

In order to achieve the rebuilding of the ground-structure of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
and to recover the content of its boxes, we introduce our notion of
connected component
(Definition
3.2
). This subsection is devoted to relate connected components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
with connected components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
(Proposition
43
and Proposition
45
).

The relation
\coh_{S}
formalizes the notion of “connectedness” between two ports of
S
at depth
0
. But be aware that, here, “connected” has nothing to do with “connected” in the sense of
[
13
]
: here, any two doors of the same box are always “connected”.

{defi}

Let
T
be a differential in-PS. We define the binary relation
\coh_{T}
on
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
as follows: for any
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, we have
p\coh_{T}p^{\prime}
iff
\{p,p^{\prime}\}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(T)\cup\mathcal{C}_{0}(T)
or
(p\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)
and
p^{\prime}=t_{\mathcal{G}(T)}(p))
or
(p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)
and
p=t_{\mathcal{G}(T)}(p^{\prime}))
or
(\exists o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T))(\exists q,q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\mathsf{f}}(o))\{p,p^{\prime}\}=\{t_{T}(o,q),t_{T}(o,q^{\prime})\}
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(T)
and let
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
. A
path in
T
from
p
to
p^{\prime}
without crossing
\mathcal{Q}
is a finite sequence
(p_{0},\ldots,p_{n})
of elements of
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
such that
p_{0}=p
,
p_{n}=p^{\prime}
and, for any
j\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
, we have
p_{j}\coh_{T}p_{j+1}
and
(p_{j}\in\mathcal{Q}\Rightarrow j=0)
.

We say that
T
is
connected through ports not in
\mathcal{Q}
if, for any
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, there exists a path in
T
from
p
to
p^{\prime}
without crossing
\mathcal{Q}
.

{defi}

Let
T
and
S
two differential in-PS’s and let
\mathcal{Q}
such that
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}}S
. We write
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}S
if the following property holds:

(\forall p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q})(\forall q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(S))(p\coh_{S}q\Rightarrow q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))

As we can expect, the connected components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
that do not cross any expanded box are exactly the connected components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
that do not cross any expanded box:

Fact 33
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
e
be an exhaustive pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
. Let
T
be a differential in-PS such that
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}}R^{\leq i}
. Then we have
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
if and only if
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
.

Proof 3.11
.

Assume
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q}
and let
q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}q
. Let us show that there is no
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
such that
p\in t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]
: Let us assume that there is such an
o
, let
e_{o}\in e(o)
(such an
e_{o}
exists since
e
is exhaustive) and let
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
such that
t_{R}(o,p^{\prime})=p
; we have
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime}))
, hence
(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, which contradicts
T\sqsubseteq R^{\leq i}
. This entails
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}q
.

Conversely, assume that
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q}
and let
q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}q
. If
q=(o,(e_{o},q^{\prime}))
for some
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
,
e_{o}\in e(o)
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(i)
such that
t_{R}(o,q^{\prime})=q
, then
p\in t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]
, hence
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}o
; then
o\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, which contradicts
T\sqsubseteq R^{\leq i}
. We thus have
q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
, hence
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}q
.

The sets
\mathcal{S}_{S}^{k}(\mathcal{Q})
of
components
T
of
S
that are connected
via
other ports than
\mathcal{Q}
, whose conclusions belong to
\mathcal{Q}
and with
\textit{co-size}(T)<k
will play a crucial role in the algorithm of the rebuilding of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
from
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
, where we will take for
\mathcal{Q}
a subset of the critical ports
\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
that were considered in the previous subsection.
31
31

31

In presence of cuts, we cannot say any more that these components are “above”
\mathcal{Q}
.
The reader already knows that, here, “connected” has nothing to do with the “connected proof-nets” of
[
13
]
: there, the crucial tool used was rather the “bridges”, which put together two doors of the same copy of some box only if they are connected in the LPS of the proof-net.

{defi}

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
S
be a differential in-PS. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(S)
. We set

\mathcal{S}_{S}^{k}(\mathcal{Q})=\left\{T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q}}S;\begin{array}[]{l}(\textit{co-size}(T)<k\textit{ and }\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq\mathcal{Q}\textit{ and}\\
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q}\not=\emptyset\textit{ and }T\textit{ is connected through ports not in $\mathcal{Q}$})\end{array}\right\}

Remark 34
.

If
T\in\mathcal{S}_{S}^{k}(\mathcal{Q})
, then
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))=\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
.

A port at depth
0
of
S
that is not in
\mathcal{Q}
cannot belong to two different components:

Fact 35
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
S
be a differential in-PS. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(S)
. Let
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{S}^{k}(\mathcal{Q})
such that
(\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime}))\setminus\mathcal{Q}\not=\emptyset
. Then
T=T^{\prime}
.

Proof 3.12
.

By Remark
7
, it is enough to check that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})
.

{exa}

We have

•

\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{S}_{S}^{10}(\{p_{1},p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{2}\})\right)=241

•

and
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{S}_{S}^{10}(\{p_{4},p_{5},p_{6},p_{7},o_{4}\})\right)=320

with
S=S1_{1}\oplus S1_{2}\oplus S1_{3}
, where
S1_{1}
,
S1_{2}
and
S1_{3}
are the differential PS’s of Figures
18
,
18
and
18
respectively.

The connected components we consider do not mix several copies of boxes. More precisely:

Lemma 36
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
. Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset
. Then the following properties hold:

(1)

\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq(t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\cap\mathcal{P})\cup\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)

(2)

\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))

(3)

\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)

Proof 3.13
.

Notice first that the two following properties hold:

(i)

For any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
, we have
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(t_{R}(o,p))\geq k
. Indeed, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
, the set
\{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{>i}(R);t_{R}(p^{\prime})=p\}
is non-empty, hence this property is obtained by applying Proposition
21
.

(ii)

For any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{P}
, we have
{\textit{a}}_{T}(p)={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(p)
.

By
(i)
and
(ii)
, we obtain

t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}\>\>\>(\ast)

Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
. There exist
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{P}
and a path
(q_{0},\ldots,q_{n})
in
T
from
q_{0}
to
p=q_{n}
without crossing
\mathcal{P}
. We set
\iota_{0}=\min\{\iota\in\{1,\ldots,n\};q_{\iota}\notin\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\}
. Since
q_{\iota_{0}}\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}q_{\iota_{0}+1}
, there exists
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
such that
q_{\iota_{0}+1}=t_{R}(o,p^{\prime})
, hence, by
(\ast)
,
q_{\iota_{0}+1}\in\mathcal{P}
; we thus have
\iota_{0}+1=n
and then
p=q_{n}\in t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\cap\mathcal{P}
. We showed Property
1
.

Property
2
is obtained by applying Property
1
and Remark
34
.

Finally, Property
3
is an immediate consequence of Properties
1
and
2
.

As a consequence, if
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
for some
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment
e
on
R
, then
T
has at most one co-contraction, which is necessarily a conclusion of
T
(hence an element of
\mathcal{P}
) and of arity
1
.

A PS
R
with cuts might have some connected components without any conclusion; copies of such components will occur in the PS
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
and we want to recover from which boxes they come from. That is why we will consider
k
-heterogeneous experiments with
k>\Theta(R)
, where
\Theta(R)
is defined as follows:

{defi}

Let
R
be an in-PS. We set

\mathcal{H}(R)=\left\{T\trianglelefteq_{\emptyset}R;\begin{array}[]{l}(\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\not=\emptyset\wedge T\textit{ is connected through ports not in $\emptyset$})\end{array}\right\}

We denote by
H_{R}
the function
\mathcal{P}(R)\to\mathcal{H}(R)
that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}(R)
the unique
T\in\mathcal{H}(R)
such that
p\in\mathcal{P}(T)
.

We define, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, the integer
\Theta(R)
as follows:

\Theta(R)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(R);\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(U)=\emptyset\}\right)+\sum_{o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)}\Theta(B_{R}(o))

Notice that, if
R
is a cut-free in-PS, then
\Theta(R)=0
.

{exa}

If
R
is the PS of Figure
11
, then
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o_{3}))\right)=4
and
\Theta(R)=1
.

The set
\mathcal{H}(R)
is an alternative way to describe an in-PS
R
:

Fact 37
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. We have
R=\bigoplus\mathcal{H}(R)
.

{defi}

Let
R
be an in-PS. We set

\beta(R)=\max\{\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R)\right),\textit{co-size}(R),\Theta(R),1\}+1

Lemma 38
.

Let
R
and
R_{o}
be two in-PS’s. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
. Let
\varphi
be a bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
such that
R_{o}=\varphi\cdot_{o}R
. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. There exists a bijection
\theta:\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})\simeq\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))\setminus\{H_{B_{R}(o)}({\oc}_{R}(o))\}
such that, for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
, we have
\theta(T)=\overline{T}
.

Proof 3.14
.

Notice first
(\forall p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R}(o)))(p\coh_{B_{R}(o)}q\Rightarrow p\coh_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}q)(\ast)
.

We check that, for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
, we have
\overline{T}\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))\setminus\{H_{B_{R}(o)}({\oc}_{R}(o))\}
; let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
:

•

By Remark
6
,
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{T})=\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\subseteq(\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))\setminus\{{\oc}_{R}(o)\})\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R}(o))\setminus\{{\oc}_{R}(o)\}
;

•

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(\overline{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(T);t_{\mathcal{G}(T)}(w)\notin\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}};t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R_{o}}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{T});t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{T})\}

•

by
(\ast)
, we have
\overline{T}\trianglelefteq_{\emptyset}B_{R}(o)

•

and
(\forall p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})(\forall q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})(p\coh_{T}q\Rightarrow p\coh_{\overline{T}}q)
, hence
\overline{T}
is connected through ports not in
\emptyset
.

Now, for any
U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))\setminus\{H_{B_{R}(o)}({\oc}_{R}(o))\}
, we set
T_{U}=(U\oplus\bigoplus_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(U)}\wn_{\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))})@t
, where
t
is the function
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(U)\to\varphi[t_{R}[\{o\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(U))]]
that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(U)
the port
\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))
of
B_{R_{o}}(o)
, and we check that
T_{U}\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
:

•

\mathcal{P}_{0}(T_{U})\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(U)\cup\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R}(o))\cup\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))

•

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T_{U})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(U)\cup(\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U);t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(U)\}\cup(\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U);t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R_{o}}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(U)\}

\displaystyle\cup\{w\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U);t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R_{o}}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T_{U})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U);t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R_{o}}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T_{U})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(U))\setminus(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(B_{R_{o}}(o));t_{\mathcal{G}(B_{R_{o}}(o))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T_{U})\}

•

\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T_{U}))=\{\varphi(t_{R}(o,p));p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(U)\}\subseteq\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}

•

\displaystyle t_{T_{U}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(U)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(U)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=8.57565pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(U)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=9.16121pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(U)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=8.57565pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=9.16121pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{B_{R}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime})\cup\{p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{B_{R}(o)}(o^{\prime}));(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\}))}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=8.57565pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=9.16121pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T_{U})}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}

•

by
(\ast)
, we have
T_{U}\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}}B_{R_{o}}(o)
and
T_{U}
is connected through ports not in
\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
.

Moreover we have
\overline{T_{U}}=U
, which shows that
\theta
is a surjection and, for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
, we have
T=(\overline{T_{U}}\oplus\bigoplus_{p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(\overline{T_{U}})}\wn_{\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))})@t
where
t
is the function
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(\overline{T_{U}})\to\varphi[t_{R}[\{o\}\times(\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(\overline{T_{U}}))]]
that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)\cap\mathcal{P}(\overline{T_{U}})
the port
\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))
of
B_{R_{o}}(o)
, which shows that
\theta
is an injection.

, where
H^{\prime}_{1}
,
H^{\prime}_{2}
and

and the differential in-PS’s of Figure

, Figure

and Figure

respectively.

Lemma 39
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
. Let
\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
be some set, let
\varphi
be some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
, let
R_{o}
be an in-PS obtained from
R
by adding, according to
\varphi
, contractions as shallow conclusions to the content of the box
o
. Then
\beta(R_{o})\leq\beta(R)
.

Proof 3.15
.

Let us show that
\Theta(R_{o})\leq\Theta(R)
:

•

If
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(H_{R_{o}}(o))=\emptyset
, then
(\{o\}\times\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{P}(H_{R_{o}}(o))=\emptyset
, hence
\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}=\emptyset
, which entails that
R_{o}=R
and then
H_{R_{o}}(o)=H_{R}(o)
.

•

Moreover we have
\{T\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R_{o}}(o));\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T)=\emptyset\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
, hence, by Lemma
38
, we have
\{T\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R_{o}}(o));\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T)=\emptyset\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))
.

Lemma 40
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
. We set
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
. Let
\varphi_{o}
be some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}
and let
R_{o}
be an in-PS such that
R_{o}=\varphi_{o}\cdot_{o}R
. Then, for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
, we have
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i}
if, and only if,
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}
.

Proof 3.16
.

Assume
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
. Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
. Notice that, since
T\sqsubseteq\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
, we have
o\notin\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)
.

Assume
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i}
. We have
T^{\leq 0}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{({R_{o}}^{\leq i})}^{\leq 0}={R_{o}}^{\leq 0}=R^{\leq 0}={({R}^{\leq i})}^{\leq 0}
. Moreover:

•

\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}({R_{o}}^{\leq i})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}^{<i}(R_{o})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}^{<i}(R)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}(R^{\leq i})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
;

•

and, since
o\notin\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)
, we have
B_{T}=\mathchoice{{B_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.82668pt,depth=2.41304pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.73334pt,depth=2.99861pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}
and
t_{T}=\mathchoice{{t_{R^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{R^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{R^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=8.57565pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{R^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=9.16121pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}
.

We obtained
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}
.

Conversely, assume
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}
. We have
T^{\leq 0}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{(R^{\leq i})}^{\leq 0}=R^{\leq 0}={R_{o}}^{\leq 0}={({R_{o}}^{\leq i})}^{\leq 0}
. Moreover:

•

\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}(R^{\leq i})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}^{<i}(R)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}^{<i}(R_{o})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{B}_{0}({R_{o}}^{\leq i})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
;

•

and, since
o\notin\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)
, we have
B_{T}=\mathchoice{{B_{R_{o}}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R_{o}}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R_{o}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.82668pt,depth=2.41304pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}{{B_{R_{o}}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.73334pt,depth=2.99861pt}}_{\,\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}}
and
t_{T}=\mathchoice{{t_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=9.39735pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=8.57565pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}{{t_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=9.16121pt}}_{\,\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(T)}(\{o^{\prime}\}\times\mathcal{P}_{{R_{o}}^{\leq i}}^{\mathsf{f}}(o^{\prime}))}}
.

We obtained
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i}
.

The proof of the following lemma is postponed in the appendix.

Lemma 41
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
. We set
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
. Let
\varphi_{o}
be some bijection
\mathcal{Q}\simeq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and let
R_{o}
be an in-PS such that
R_{o}=\varphi_{o}\cdot_{o}R
. Let
\varphi_{e_{o}}
be the bijection
\mathcal{Q}\simeq\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{Q^{\prime}})
defined by
\varphi_{e_{o}}(p)=(o,(e_{o},\varphi_{o}(p)))
for any
p\in\mathcal{Q}
. We set
\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}=(\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Q})\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{Q}]
. Then:

(1)

for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset
, we have
T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}})
;

(2)

for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}})
such that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
, we have
T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
;

(3)

for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
, we have
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
;

(4)

for any
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
, we have
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
;

The set of “critical components” we will consider in our algorithm is the set

\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))

From this set we will build the contents of the new boxes. In particular, each port of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
at depth
0
that goes inside a new box of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
(i.e. each element of
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
) belongs to a “critical component”:

Lemma 42
.

Let
R
be a PS. Let
k>\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R)\right),\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
, there exists
T\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
such that

p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))

Proof 3.17
.

By induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
. If
\textit{depth}(R)=0
, then
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))=\emptyset
. Assume that
\textit{depth}(R)>0
and let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
. There exist
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
. We distinguish between two cases:

•

o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
: By Proposition
31
, there exists
j_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
such that
\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})=o
. We have
R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle=\langle o,\langle e_{o},B_{R}(o)\rangle\rangle
, hence there exists
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))
such that
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle)
. We set
T=(\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle\oplus\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})}{l_{\mathcal{G}(R)}(t_{R}(o,p))}_{t_{R}(o,p)})@\mathchoice{{t_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})}}{{t_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})}}{{t_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=6.45059pt}}_{\,\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})}}{{t_{R}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=7.03615pt}}_{\,\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})}}
. We have
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))=t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})]=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T^{\prime})]\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
(by Proposition
31
), hence
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
.

•

o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
: There exists
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o}))
such that
p=(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime}))
. Let
R_{o}
be an in-PS such that
R_{o}=\varphi\cdot_{o}R
, where
\varphi
is some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
. We have
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o}))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o}))
, hence, by induction hypothesis, there exists
T^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
such that
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})
; let
j_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
such that
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
. By Corollary
22
, we have
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}((\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})))\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
. By Corollary
32
, we have
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\varphi[\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\cap\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))]\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
. We denote by
\varphi_{e_{o}}
the bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
that associates
(o,(e_{o},\varphi(q)))
with every
q\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
. By Proposition
31
, we have
\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\cap\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))]\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
. We set
T=\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]
. By Lemma
41
(
2
), we have
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}((\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\cap\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
; we thus have
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
.

Notice that not each port belonging to a “critical component” goes inside a new box. That is why, for now, we are not yet able to describe exactly the ground-structure of
{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}
but we can only obtain an approximant of
{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\leq 0}
:

Proposition 43
.

Let
R
be a PS. Let
k>\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R)\right),\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. We set

\mathcal{P}=\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}\bigcup_{\begin{array}[]{c}T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\end{array}}\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\right)\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))

Then we have
\mathchoice{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=7.14178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.74178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.29153pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.1851pt,depth=1.44028pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}\sqsubseteq_{\emptyset}{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\leq 0}
.

Proof 3.18
.

By Lemma
23
, we have:

(1)

\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
and
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}=\mathchoice{{t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}}{{t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.92064pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}}{{t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44446pt,depth=2.57834pt}}_{\,\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}}{{t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.46031pt,depth=3.1639pt}}_{\,\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}}

(2)

\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))

By
1
., we have

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

By Lemma
42
and Proposition
31
, we have
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))

(\ast)
. By
(\ast)
and
2
., we have

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

We thus showed

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\mathchoice{{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.44028pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\{w\in\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)))\cap\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}\}

There is no other connected component of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
whose conclusions belong to critical ports of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
than critical components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
:

Proposition 44
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
k>\textit{co-size}(R)
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
.

Proof 3.19
.

Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
. Notice that
\textit{depth}(T)<i
. We have

T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),

hence, by Remark
3
, Lemma
23
and Fact
10
,
T=T^{\leq i}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
.

If
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}R^{\leq i}
, then one can apply Fact
33
.

Otherwise: Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
and
q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}q
. By Lemma
42
, there exists
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
such that
p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
, which contradicts
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
.

We will apply Proposition
45
with
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
, where
j_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, but, since the proof is by induction, we need to slightly generalize it.

Proposition 45
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
k\geq\beta(R)
.
Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
. Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))
. We set

•

\mathcal{T}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T\equiv T^{\prime}\}

•

\mathcal{T^{\prime}}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T\equiv T^{\prime}\}

•

\mathcal{B}=\{o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R);\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\not=\emptyset\}

•

\mathcal{B^{\prime}}=\{o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R);\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\not=\emptyset\}

Let
(m_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}},(m^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m_{j}\cdot k^{j}

and
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}
. Then the following properties hold:

•

\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)

•

\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)

•

(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e))m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}

•

(\forall j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e))m_{j}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

Moreover, if
\mathcal{B}\not=\emptyset
, then the following properties hold:

•

\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq\mathcal{B}\cup\bigcup_{o\in\mathcal{B}}\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})]

•

\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq\mathcal{B^{\prime}}\cup\bigcup_{o\in\mathcal{B^{\prime}}}\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})]

Finally, if
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, then
m_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)=m^{\prime}_{0}
.

Proof 3.20
.

We prove the proposition by induction on
\left(\textit{depth}(R),\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\right)
lexicographically ordered. Part I) is devoted to the case where
\textit{depth}(R)=0
, Part II) is devoted to the case where
\textit{depth}(R)>0
and
\mathcal{B}=\emptyset
, Part III) is devoted to the case where
\textit{depth}(R)>0
,
\mathcal{B}\not=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)=\emptyset
, and Part IV) is devoted to the case where
\textit{depth}(R)>0
,
\mathcal{B}\not=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)\not=\emptyset
.

Part I

\textit{depth}(R)=0
: Then
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)=R=\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
and
R^{\leq i}=R
, hence
\mathcal{T}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}=\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
;

•

\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\not=\emptyset
:
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)\leq\textit{co-size}(R)<k
;

•

\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))=\emptyset
:
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)\leq\Theta(R)<k
;

so, in both cases,
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)<k
, which entails
m_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)=m^{\prime}_{0}
and
\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m_{j}\not=0\}=\emptyset=\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}
; moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)=\emptyset=\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
.

Part II

\textit{depth}(R)>0
and
\mathcal{B}=\emptyset
:
32
32

32

As an instance of this case, take for
R
the PS that is depicted in Figure
11
, take
i=0
, take for
e
a
10
-heterogeneous experiment with
e^{\#}
like in Example
2
, take for
\mathcal{P}
any subset of
\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[1](e))\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e))
and for
T
one of the
10^{224}
copies of

[rgb]0,0,0
[formula]
[rgb]0,0,0
[formula]

.
Then we distinguish between two cases (Case 1) and Case 2)):

•

Case 1) There exist
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that the set
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
is non-empty: By Lemma
36
, we have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
; we set
\mathcal{P}_{o}=\{p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}));(o,(e_{o},p))\in\mathcal{P}\}
and
T_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{o})
such that
T=\langle o,\langle e_{o},T_{0}\rangle\rangle
; we have
\mathcal{T}=\{\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle;(T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{o})\wedge T_{0}\equiv T^{\prime})\}
and
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}=\{\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle;(T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{o})\wedge T_{0}\equiv T^{\prime})\}
. We have
\beta(B_{R}(o))\leq\beta(R)
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T_{0})\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T_{0}))
, hence we can apply the induction hypothesis: we obtain

–

\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)
(by Lemma
28
)

–

\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)
(by Lemma
28
)

–

and
(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o}))m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
, which entails
(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e))m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
, since, by Lemma
28
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)\cap\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})=\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})
.

Now, let
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, let
U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))
such that
U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T
and let
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(U))
; there exists
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))
such that
p^{\prime}=t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j),p)
; by Proposition
31
, we have
\oc_{e,i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
or there exist
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
and
e_{o^{\prime}}\in e(o^{\prime})
such that
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o^{\prime}})
:

–

\oc_{e,i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
: then we have
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, hence
p^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{P}
, which is in contradiction with
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq\mathcal{P}
;

–

or (
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o^{\prime}})
for some
o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
and
e_{o^{\prime}}\in e(o^{\prime})
): then we have

p^{\prime}=(o^{\prime},(e_{o^{\prime}},t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o^{\prime})}[i+1](e_{o^{\prime}})}(\oc_{e_{o^{\prime}},i}(j),p)))\in\mathcal{P}

hence
(o^{\prime},e_{o^{\prime}})=(o,e_{o})
.

This shows that, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)\setminus\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
, we have

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)=0

but, since
{j}\notin\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})
, we have
m_{j}=0
.

For any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})
, we have
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
; applying the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e,i}(j))));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))))}T\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o}),\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))}T_{0}\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{j}

•

Case 2) For any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
, for any
e_{o}\in e(o)
, we have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)=\emptyset
: Notice that then
T\sqsubseteq R
. We set
\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
. We have
\mathcal{T}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}R^{\leq i}\}
,
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}R^{\leq i}\}
and
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)<k
, hence, by Fact
33
, we have
m_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)=m^{\prime}_{0}
and
\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m_{j}\not=0\}=\emptyset=\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}
.

Since
T\sqsubseteq R
and
\mathcal{B}=\emptyset
, for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))
, we have

\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),o)}T\}=\emptyset

Part III

\textit{depth}(R)>0
,
\mathcal{B}\not=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)=\emptyset
:
33
33

33

As an instance of this case, take for
R
the PS that is depicted in Figure
11
, take
i=1
, take for
e
a
10
-heterogeneous experiment with
e^{\#}
like in Example
2
, take
\mathcal{P}=\{p_{1}\}
and for
T
the differential PS that is depicted in Figure
34
(see Example
3.2
on page
3.2
).
Then let
o\in\mathcal{B}
: Let
R_{o}
be an in-PS and
\varphi
be some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
such that
R_{o}=\varphi\cdot_{o}R
.

Roughly speaking, the number
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m_{j}\cdot k^{j}
(resp.
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}
) of components of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
(resp.
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
) that are equivalent to the connected component
T
is the sum of the number
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p_{j}\cdot k^{j}
(resp.
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}
) of such components that come from the expansion of the box
o
and the number
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}n_{j}\cdot k^{j}
(resp.
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}n^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}
) of such components that do not come from the expansion of the box
o
, but we define the sequence
(n_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
(resp. the sequence
(n^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
) through
\mathcal{T}_{\varphi\cdot_{o}R}[i](e)
(resp.
\mathcal{T}_{\varphi\cdot_{o}R}[i+1](e)
), and not through
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
(resp.
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
), in order to be able to apply the induction hypothesis.

Let
j_{o}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)
such that
\oc_{e,i}(j_{o})=o
. We define a subset
\mathcal{N}
of
\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
as follows: We set
\mathcal{N}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{j_{o}\}&\textit{if $o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)$;}\\
\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{N}_{i}(e_{o})&\textit{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.
For every
e_{o}\in e(o)
, let
\varphi_{e_{o}}
be the bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
defined by
\varphi_{e_{o}}(q)=(o,(e_{o},\varphi(q)))
for any
q\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
; we set
\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}=(\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o))\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)]
.

Let
(p_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p_{j}\cdot k^{j}

For every
e_{o}\in e(o)
, let
(p_{e_{o},j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

Let
(p^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

•

if
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
, then

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i+1,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}

•

if
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then
0=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}
.

For any
e_{o}\in e(o)
, let
(p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

•

if
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
, then

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

•

if
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then
0=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}
.

By Lemma
36
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e_{o}}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

hence, by Lemma
41
(
1
) and (
2
), we have:

•

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p_{j}\cdot k^{j}=\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

•

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}=\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

Let
(n_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T)));T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}n_{j}\cdot k^{j}

and let
(n^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T)));T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}n^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}.

By Lemma
41
(
3
) and (
4
), we have:

•

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m_{j}\cdot k^{j}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(p_{j}+n_{j})\cdot k^{j}

•

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot k^{j}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(p^{\prime}_{j}+n^{\prime}_{j})\cdot k^{j}

Now, the proof will be in three steps (Step 1), Step 2) and Step 3)):

Step 1)

It consists in proving properties about the sequences
(n_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(n^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. We distinguish between two cases:

•

\mathcal{B}=\{o\}
: Then
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T^{\prime}\equiv T\}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});(T^{\prime}\equiv T\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i})\}
and
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T^{\prime}\equiv T\}=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});(T^{\prime}\equiv T\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i})\}
, hence
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)<k
and, by Fact
33
, we have

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)

so
\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};n_{j}\not=0\}=\emptyset=\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};n^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}
and

n_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});(T^{\prime}\equiv T\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i})\}\right)=n^{\prime}_{0}

(in particular,
(\forall j\in\mathbb{N})n^{\prime}_{j}=n_{j}
). If
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, then, by Lemma
40
, we have
n_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)=n^{\prime}_{0}
.

•

\mathcal{B}\setminus\{o\}\not=\emptyset
: By Lemma
39
, we can apply the induction hypothesis: We have

–

\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};n_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq(\mathcal{B}\setminus\{o\})\cup\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\setminus\{o\}}\bigcup_{e_{o^{\prime}}\in e(o^{\prime})}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o^{\prime}})]

–

\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};n^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq(\mathcal{B^{\prime}}\setminus\{o\})\cup\bigcup_{o^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B^{\prime}}\setminus\{o\}}\bigcup_{e_{o^{\prime}}\in e(o^{\prime})}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o^{\prime}})]

–

(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e))n^{\prime}_{j}=n_{j}

–

if
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, then
n_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P});(T^{\prime}\equiv T\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}{R_{o}}^{\leq i})\}\right)=n^{\prime}_{0}
, hence, by Lemma
40
, we have
n_{0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}\right)=n^{\prime}_{0}
.

Now, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)\setminus\mathcal{N}
, we have
B_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j))=B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j))
, hence

\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)=n_{j}

Step 2)

In order to prove

•

\{j\in\mathbb{N};p_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\{0,j_{o}\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})

•

(p_{0}\not=0\Rightarrow n_{0}=0)

•

\{j\in\mathbb{N};p^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\{0,j_{o}\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})

•

(o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)\Rightarrow(\forall j\in\{j_{o}\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o}))p^{\prime}_{j}=p_{j})

•

(p^{\prime}_{0}\not=0\Rightarrow n^{\prime}_{0}=0)

•

(\forall j\in\mathcal{N})p_{j}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

we distinguish between three cases (Case a), Case b) and Case c)):

•

Case a)
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
: It is worth noticing that the differential in-PS
T
has no co-contraction. If there is no
e_{1}\in e(o)
such that
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{1}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]\}\not=\emptyset
, then, by Lemma
41
(
1
), for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
p_{j}=0=p^{\prime}_{j}
, so there is nothing to prove. From now on, let us assume that there exist
e_{1}\in e(o)
and
T^{\prime}_{1}\in\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{1}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]\}
; let
\zeta:T^{\prime}_{1}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]
and let
T_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
such that
T^{\prime}_{1}=\langle o,\langle e_{1},T_{1}\rangle\rangle
; notice that, for any
