, we have
T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\equiv\langle o,\langle e_{o},T_{1}\rangle\rangle
, hence

\displaystyle\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}\rangle\rangle;(T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\wedge T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1})\}\>\>\>(\ast)

we have
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T_{1}))\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R_{o}}(o))
, hence, since by Lemma
39
we can apply the induction hypothesis, we have

p_{e_{1},0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});(T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\leq i})\}\right)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\}\right)\>\>\>\textit{(by $(\ast)$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});(T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{\leq i})\}\right)\cdot\mathsf{Card}\left(e(o)\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})}p_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

hence
\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N};p_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq\{o\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})]
.

If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then there exists a bijection
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\}\simeq\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),o)}T\}
that associates with every
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
such that
T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}
the differential in-PS
\overline{T^{\prime}}
. Indeed:

–

By Lemma
38
, there exists a bijection
\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\simeq\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o))
that associates with every
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
the differential in-PS
\overline{T^{\prime}}
;

–

If
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
and
\psi:T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}
, then the function
\delta
that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}(\overline{T^{\prime}})
the port
\zeta(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(p)))
of
\overline{T}
is an isomorphism
\overline{T^{\prime}}\simeq\overline{T}
such that,
for any
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\overline{T^{\prime}})
, we have
\mathfrak{t}_{T^{\prime}}(p)=\mathfrak{t}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}(p)=\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))
, hence

\displaystyle\varphi_{e_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}_{T}(\delta(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{e_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}_{T}(\zeta(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}_{T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]}(\zeta(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\zeta(\mathfrak{t}_{T^{\prime}_{1}}(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}_{T^{\prime}_{1}}(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o_{1},(e_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{T_{1}}(\psi(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o_{1},(e_{1},\psi(\mathfrak{t}_{T^{\prime}}(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o_{1},(e_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{T^{\prime}}(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o_{1},(e_{1},\varphi(t_{R}(o,p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{e_{1}}(t_{R}(o,p))

So,

t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o,p)=t_{R}(o,p)=\mathfrak{t}_{T}(\delta(p))
.

If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
, then, by induction hypothesis,
p^{\prime}_{e_{1},0}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}B_{R_{o}}(o);T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\}\right)
and

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});(T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}B_{R_{o}}(o))\}\right)\cdot\mathsf{Card}\left(e(o)\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})}p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j}\cdot k^{j}

hence
\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N};p^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\{o\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})]
; by induction hypothesis, for any
e_{o}\in e(o)
, we have
(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})\cup\{0\})p^{\prime}_{e_{o},j}=p_{e_{o},j}
; moreover, we have

p_{j_{o}}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime});(T^{\prime}\equiv T_{1}\wedge T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}B_{R_{o}}(o))\}\right)=p^{\prime}_{j_{o}}

now, let
j\in\mathcal{N}
and let
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
: We have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{j}\textit{ (by induction hypothesis)}

•

Case b) There exists
e_{1}\in e(o)
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)\not=\emptyset
: By Lemma
36
, we have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)\cup t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]
, hence
(\forall j\in\mathbb{N})n_{j}=0=n^{\prime}_{j}
; we set
\mathcal{P^{\prime}}=\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\cup\{p;(o,(e_{1},p))\in\mathcal{P}\}
; let
T_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{P^{\prime}})
such that
T=\langle o,\langle e_{1},T_{0}\rangle\rangle
; we have
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(T_{0})\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T_{0}))
and, by Lemma
39
,
\beta(R_{o})\leq\beta(R)\leq k
, hence we can apply the induction hypothesis: We obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{1}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{P^{\prime}});T^{\prime}\equiv T_{0}\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{1})\cup\{0\}}p_{e_{1},j}\cdot k^{j}

hence we have
\{j\in\mathbb{N};p_{j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{1})\cup\{0\}
.

If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
then
\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{1})=\emptyset
, hence
p_{j_{o}}=0
; from the other hand, since
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)\not=\emptyset
, we have
\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{o})));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}=\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(o));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}=\emptyset
.

If
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
, then, by induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i+1,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i+1,e_{1}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{1}});T^{\prime}\equiv T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{1})}^{k}(\mathcal{P^{\prime}});T^{\prime}\equiv T_{0}\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{1})\cup\{0\}}p^{\prime}_{e_{1},j}\cdot k^{j}

and
\{j\in\mathbb{N};p^{\prime}_{e_{1},j}\not=0\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{1})\cup\{0\}
and
(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{1})\cup\{0\})p^{\prime}_{e_{1},j}=p_{e_{1},j}
; let
j\in\mathcal{N}
and let
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
:

–

if
e_{o}\not=e_{1}
, then
j\notin\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{1})
, hence
p_{j}=0
; from the other hand, we have

\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}=\emptyset

–

if
e_{o}=e_{1}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T[\varphi_{e_{1}}]\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{j}\textit{ (by induction hypothesis)}

•

Case c)
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap(\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)\setminus\{t_{R}(o,p);p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(o))\})\not=\emptyset
: By Lemma
36
, for any
e_{o}\in e(o)
, we have
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}=\emptyset=\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}};\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset\}
, hence
(\forall j\in\mathbb{N})p_{j}=0=p^{\prime}_{j}
. From the other hand, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, the set
\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}
is empty.

Step 3)

We distinguish between two cases:

•

Case a)
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
: We have
\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N};p_{j}\not=0\}\cap\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)]\subseteq\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N};p_{j}\not=0\}]\cap\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)]\subseteq(\{o\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})])\cap\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)]=(\{o\}\cap\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)])\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}(\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})]\cap\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)])\subseteq\emptyset\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i}(e_{o})\cap\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)]=\emptyset
, hence, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
m_{j}=n_{j}
; by Lemma
41
(
3
) and(
4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T)));T^{\prime}\equiv T\}\right)

hence, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
m^{\prime}_{j}=n^{\prime}_{j}
; since, for any
j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)
, we have
n^{\prime}_{j}=n_{j}
, we obtain
(\forall j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e))m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
.

•

Case b)
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
: We have
\{j\in\mathbb{N};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}=\{j\in\mathbb{N};n^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}\cup\{j\in\mathbb{N};p^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}
, hence
\oc_{e,i}[\{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\};m^{\prime}_{j}\not=0\}]\subseteq\mathcal{B^{\prime}}\cup\bigcup_{o\in\mathcal{B^{\prime}}}\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\oc_{e,i}[\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})]
. For any
j\in\{j_{o}\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o})
, we have
n^{\prime}_{j}=n_{j}
and
p^{\prime}_{j}=p_{j}
, hence
m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
; for any
j\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e)\setminus(\{j_{o}\}\cup\bigcup_{e_{o}\in e(o)}\mathcal{M}_{i+1}(e_{o}))
, we have
n^{\prime}_{j}=n_{j}
and
p^{\prime}_{j}=0=p_{0}
, hence
m^{\prime}_{j}=m_{j}
.

Part IV

\textit{depth}(R)>0
,
\mathcal{B}\not=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)\not=\emptyset
: Let
o\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\cap\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
. There exists
e_{o}\in e(o)
such that
(o,(e_{o},{\oc}_{R}(o)))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
and
t_{T}(o,(e_{o},{\oc}_{R}(o)))=o
. Notice that
o
is a co-contraction of
T
such that
{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{G}(T)}(o)>0
, which entails that the set
\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T};T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}R^{\leq i}\}
is empty. We distinguish between two cases: Case a), where
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]
and Case b), where
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\setminus t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\not=\emptyset
.

•

Case a)
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]
: Let
j_{o}\in\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)
such that
\oc_{e,i}(j_{o})=o
.

–

We have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=k^{j_{o}}
.

–

We have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}k^{j_{o}}&\textit{if $o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)$;}\\
0&\textit{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

–

We have
\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{o})));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j_{o}))}T\}=H_{B_{R}(o)}({\oc}_{R}(o))
, hence, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Card}\left(\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\textit{if $j=j_{o}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)$;}\\
0&\textit{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

•

Case b)
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\setminus t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\not=\emptyset
: By Lemma
36
,
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus t_{R}[\{o\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)]\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
, hence
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
does not hold.

–

For any
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
, we have
(o,(e_{o},{\oc}_{R}(o)))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})
, hence
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)\leq 1
and
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T^{\prime}}\right)\leq 1
.

–

For any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, we have

\displaystyle\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)));U\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T\}=\emptyset

3.3.
The content of the boxes

Lemma 46
.

Let
S
be a differential in-PS. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
. Let
\mathcal{T}
be a set of differential in-PS’s that is gluable. Assume that there exists a bijection
\gamma:\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o))\simeq\mathcal{T}
such that, for any
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o))
, we have
V\equiv_{(S,o)}\gamma(V)
. Then
B_{S}(o)\equiv_{(S,o)}\bigoplus\mathcal{T}
.

Proof 3.21
.

For each
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o))
, we are given
\varphi_{V}:V\equiv_{(S,o)}\gamma(V)
. Now, we define
\varphi:\bigoplus\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o))\equiv_{(S,o)}\bigoplus\mathcal{T}
as follows: for any
p\in\mathcal{P}(\bigoplus\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o)))
, we set
\varphi(p)=\varphi_{H_{B_{S}(o)}(p)}(p)
. But, by Fact
37
, we have
\bigoplus\mathcal{H}(B_{S}(o))=B_{S}(o)
.

Fact 47
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i}(R)
. Let
e_{o}\in e(o)
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
o_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))
. Let
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})}(o_{1}))
and let
T
be an in-PS such that
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}),o_{1})}T
. Then
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e),(o,(e_{o},o_{1})))}\langle o,\langle e_{o},T\rangle\rangle
.

Proof 3.22
.

Let
\varphi:V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}),o_{1})}T
. Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be the function
\mathcal{P}(V)\to\mathcal{P}(\langle o,\langle e_{o},T\rangle\rangle)
defined by
\varphi^{\prime}(p)=(o,(e_{o},\varphi(p)))
for any
p
.
For any
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(V)
, we have

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}((o,(e_{o},o_{1})),p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o,(e_{o},t_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o})}(o_{1},p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(o,(e_{o},t_{T}(\varphi(p))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\langle o,\langle e_{o},T\rangle\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime}(p))

We thus have
\varphi^{\prime}:V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e),(o,(e_{o},o_{1})))}\langle o,\langle e_{o},T\rangle\rangle
.

The rebuilding of the content of the boxes of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
is achieved by the following proposition:

Proposition 48
.

Let
R
be a PS. Let
k\geq\beta(R)
.
Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
j_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
.
We set
\mathfrak{T}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))/\equiv
. For any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, let
(m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}}\cdot k^{j}
. Let
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j_{0}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
such that, for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, we have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{T}\right)=m_{j_{0}}^{\mathcal{T}}
. Then we have

B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0}))\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j_{0}))}\bigoplus\mathcal{U}

Proof 3.23
.

We first prove, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, that, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
, there exists an injection
\xi:\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
such that, for any
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))
, we have
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}\xi(V)
.
(\ast)

Let
j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
and let
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))
.

If
\oc_{e,i}(j)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{=i}(R)
, then
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))
: Let
e_{1}\in e(\oc_{e,i}(j))
. Let
\xi(V)
be the following differential PS:

\xi(V)=(\langle\oc_{e,i}(j),\langle e_{1},V\rangle\rangle\oplus\bigoplus_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\cap\mathcal{P}(V)}l_{\mathcal{G}(R)}(t_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j),p))_{t_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j),p)})@t

where
t
is the function
\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times(\{e_{1}\}\times(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\cap\mathcal{P}(V)))\to t_{R}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\cap\mathcal{P}(V))]
that associates with every
(\oc_{e,i}(j),(e_{1},p))
for some
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\cap\mathcal{P}(V)
the port
t_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j),p)
of
\mathcal{G}(R)
.
By Proposition
31
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(\xi(V)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{R}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))\cap\mathcal{P}(V))]

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle t_{R}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(\oc_{e,i}(j)))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{R}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}[\{\oc_{e,i}(j)\}\times\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\mathsf{f}}(\oc_{e,i}(j))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))

hence
\xi(V)\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
. Moreover, we have
\overline{\xi(V)}=\langle\oc_{e,i}(j),\langle e_{1},V\rangle\rangle

and
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}\xi(V)
.

Now, if
\oc_{e,i}(j)=(o,(e_{o},\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
for some
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\geq i+1}(R)
and
e_{o}\in e(o)
, then we have
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
: Let
\varphi
be some bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and let
R_{o}
be an in-PS such that
R_{o}=\varphi\cdot_{o}R
; we have
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o})}(\oc_{e_{o},i}(j)))
, hence, by induction hypothesis, there exists
T^{\prime}_{V}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i](e_{o})))
such that
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R_{o}}(o)}[i+1](e_{o}),\oc_{e_{o},i}(j))}T^{\prime}_{V}
. Let
\varphi_{e_{o}}
be the bijection
\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)\simeq\{o\}\times(\{e_{o}\}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
defined by
\varphi_{e_{o}}(p)=(o,(e_{o},\varphi(p)))
for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)
. We have
\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}_{V}\rangle\rangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}((\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o))\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\wn}(o)])
, hence, by Lemma
41
(
2
),
\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}_{V}\rangle\rangle[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
. Moreover, by Fact
47
, we have
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}_{V}\rangle\rangle
, hence
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}_{V}\rangle\rangle[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]
. We can thus set
\xi(V)=\langle o,\langle e_{o},T^{\prime}_{V}\rangle\rangle[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]
.

We proved
(\ast)
. Now, let us show that there exists a bijection

\gamma:\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})))\simeq\mathcal{U}

such that, for any
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})))
, we have
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j_{0}))}\gamma(V)
: For any
T\in\mathsf{im}(\xi)
, by Proposition
45
, there exists a bijection

\gamma_{T}:\{U\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})));U\equiv_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})}T\}\simeq\{T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U};T^{\prime}\equiv T\}

We define the function
\gamma
by setting
\gamma(V)=\gamma_{\xi(V)}(V)
for any
V
. Since
\xi
is an injection,
\gamma
is an injection. Let us check that
\gamma
is a surjection too: Let
T\in\mathcal{U}
; by Proposition
45
, there exists
V\in\mathcal{H}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0})))
such that
V\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j))}T
; by Remark
14
, we have
T\in\mathsf{im}(\gamma_{\xi(V)})
.

Finally, we can apply Lemma
46
to obtain
B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc_{e,i}(j_{0}))\equiv_{(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e),\oc_{e,i}(j_{0}))}\bigoplus\mathcal{U}
.

{exa}

Consider the
3
-heterogeneous experiment
e
on the PS’s
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
(which are depicted in Figure

and in Figure

respectively) such that
e^{\#}(o_{1})=\{3\}
and
e^{\#}(o_{2})=\{9\}
. We set
\mathfrak{T}_{1}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{1}}[0](e)}^{3}(\mathcal{K}_{3,1}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{1}}[0](e)))/\equiv
and
\mathfrak{T}_{2}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{2}}[0](e)}^{3}(\mathcal{K}_{3,1}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{2}}[0](e)))/\equiv
. We have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathfrak{T}_{1}\right)=3=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathfrak{T}_{2}\right)
.

,

,

3.4.
Characterizing a PS by some finite subset of its Taylor expansion

The proof of the following proposition contains a full description of the algorithm leading from
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
to
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
:

Proposition 49
.

Let
R
and
R^{\prime}
be two PS’s. Let
k\geq\max\{\beta(R),\beta(R^{\prime})\}
. Let
e
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
and let
e^{\prime}
be a
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e)\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](e^{\prime})
. Then, for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})
.

Proof 3.24
.

By induction on
i
. We assume that we are given
\varphi:\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})
.

We set
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{i}(e)
(resp.
\mathcal{M^{\prime}}=\mathcal{M}_{i}(e^{\prime})
) and
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{i}(e)
(resp.
\mathcal{N^{\prime}}=\mathcal{N}_{i}(e^{\prime})
). There is a bijection
\oc:\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})
(resp. a bijection
\oc^{\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime}))\setminus\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})})
) such that, for any
j\in\mathcal{M}
, we have
({\textit{a}}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}}\circ\oc)(j)=k^{j}
(resp.
({\textit{a}}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}}\circ\oc^{\prime})(j)=k^{j}
). For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we set
\mathcal{T}_{j}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
(resp.
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})))
). We set
\mathfrak{T}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{T}_{j}/\equiv)
(resp.
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}/\equiv)
). We set
\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\bigcup_{\begin{array}[]{c}T\in\mathcal{T}_{j}\end{array}}\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})
(resp.
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{P}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N^{\prime}}}\bigcup_{\begin{array}[]{c}T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}\end{array}}\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime}))\cup\mathcal{K}_{k,\mathcal{N^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})})
). For any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
(resp.
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
), we define
(m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}})\in{\{0,\ldots,k-1\}}^{\mathbb{N}}
(resp.
(n_{j}^{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})\in{\{0,\ldots,k-1\}}^{\mathbb{N}}
) as follows:
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}}\cdot k^{j}
(resp.
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}n_{j}^{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\cdot k^{j}
).

Notice that
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
and, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}
,
\varphi[\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))]=\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime}))
. Moreover, there exists a bijection
\sigma:\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j}\simeq\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}
such that, for any
T\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j}
, there is an isomorphism
T\simeq\sigma(T)
associating with every port
p
of
{T}
the port
\varphi(p)
of
\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})
, hence
\varphi[\mathcal{P}]=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. Also, notice that, for any
T_{1},T_{2}\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j}
, we have
(T_{1}\equiv T_{2}\Leftrightarrow\sigma(T_{1})\equiv\sigma(T_{2}))
, which entails that there exists a bijection
\overline{\sigma}:\mathfrak{T}\simeq\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
such that, for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, for any
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
, for any
T\in\mathcal{T}
, for any
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
, we have
(\sigma(T)=T^{\prime}\Rightarrow\overline{\sigma}(\mathcal{T})=\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
; moreover, for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, we have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\overline{\sigma}(\mathcal{T})\right)
. For any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, we thus have
(n_{j}^{\overline{\sigma}(\mathcal{T})})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}=(m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
.

For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we set
\mathcal{V}_{j}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)))
. We set
\mathfrak{V}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{V}_{j}/\equiv)
. For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we are given
\mathcal{U}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{j}
such that, for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathcal{T}_{j}/\equiv
, we have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{U}_{j}\cap\mathcal{T}\right)=m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}}
. Then one can describe the differential PS
\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)
as follows:

•

\mathchoice{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=7.14178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.74178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.29153pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.1851pt,depth=1.44028pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}}}\sqsubseteq_{\emptyset}{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\leq 0}
(by Proposition
43
);

•

{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}^{\leq i}\sqsubseteq_{\emptyset}{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}
(by Lemma
23
);

•

(\forall j\in\mathcal{N})\mathcal{V}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{j}
(by Proposition
44
);

•

for any
\mathcal{V}\in\mathfrak{V}
, we have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)=\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}}m_{j}^{\mathcal{T}}\cdot k^{j}
, where
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
such that
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{T}
(by Proposition
45
);

•

\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)})=(\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}))\cup\oc[\mathcal{N}]
(by Lemma
23
and Proposition
31
);

•

and, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, there exists
\varphi_{j}:B_{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}}(\oc(j))\equiv_{({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)},\oc(j))}\bigoplus\mathcal{U}_{j}
(by Proposition
48
).

In the same way: For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we set
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}=\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})))
. We set
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}/\equiv)
. For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we set
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{j}=\sigma[\mathcal{U}_{j}]
. Then one can describe the differential PS
\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})
as follows:

•

\mathchoice{{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=7.14178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P^{\prime}}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.74178pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P^{\prime}}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.29153pt,depth=1.53944pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P^{\prime}}}}{{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}^{\leq 0}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.1851pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P^{\prime}}}}\sqsubseteq_{\emptyset}{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}^{\leq 0}
(by Proposition
43
);

•

{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}^{\leq i}\sqsubseteq_{\emptyset}{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}
(by Lemma
23
);

•

(\forall j\in\mathcal{N^{\prime}})\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}
(by Proposition
44
);

•

for any
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)=\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}n_{j}^{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\cdot k^{j}
, where
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
(by Proposition
45
);

•

\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})})=(\mathcal{B}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})})\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}))\cup\oc^{\prime}[\mathcal{N}^{\prime}]
(by Lemma
23
and Proposition
31
);

•

and, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
, there exists
\varphi^{\prime}_{j}:B_{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}}(\oc^{\prime}(j))\equiv_{({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})},\oc^{\prime}(j))}\bigoplus\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{j}
(by Proposition
48
).

So, for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{T}
, there exists a bijection
\tau_{\mathcal{T}}:\mathcal{T}\simeq\overline{\sigma}(\mathcal{T})
such that

\tau_{\mathcal{T}}[\mathcal{T}\cap(\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{S}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})))]=\overline{\sigma}(\mathcal{T})\cap(\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})})))

hence there exist a bijection
\tau:\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j}\simeq\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}
and a sequence
(\psi_{T})_{T\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j}}
such that
(\forall T\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}_{j})\psi_{T}:\sigma(T)\equiv\tau(T)
and

\tau[\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{S}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}({\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}))]=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N^{\prime}}}\mathcal{S}_{{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}}^{k}(\mathcal{K}_{k,j}({\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime})}))

For any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime}))\setminus\mathcal{P^{\prime}}
, let
H(p)
be the unique
T^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{j}
such that
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T^{\prime})
.

We can thus define a bijection
\psi:\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\simeq\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))
such that:

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
\psi(p)=\varphi(p)\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\cap\mathcal{P}
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o))
, we have
\psi(o,p)=\varphi(o,p)
;

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}
, we have
\psi(p)=\psi_{H(\varphi(p))}(\varphi(p))
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}^{<i}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(o))
, we have
\psi(o,p)=\psi_{H(\varphi(o))}(\varphi(o,p))
;

•

and, for any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}(B_{{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}}(\oc(j)))
, we have
\psi(\oc(j),p)=(\varphi(\oc(j)),({\varphi^{\prime}_{j}}^{-1}\circ\varphi\circ\varphi_{j})(p))
.

It is straightforward to check that
\psi:\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})
. In particular:

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}
such that
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(p)\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
\varphi(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p))\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(H(\varphi(p)))=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(H(\varphi(p)))
, hence

\displaystyle\psi(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(p))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))}(p))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{H(\varphi(p))}(\varphi(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{H(\varphi(p))}(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime}))}(\varphi(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i](e^{\prime}))}(\psi_{H(\varphi(p))}(\varphi(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))}(\psi_{H(\varphi(p))}(\varphi(p)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))}(\psi(p))

•

we have

\displaystyle\psi[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\cap\mathcal{P}]\cup\psi[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi[\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\cap\mathcal{P}]\cup\{\psi_{H(\varphi(o))}(\varphi(o));o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e))\setminus\mathcal{P}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))\cap\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\cup(\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime}))

•

For any
j\in\mathcal{N}
, we have an isomorphism
\varphi_{\oc(j)}:\bigoplus\mathcal{U}_{j}\simeq\bigoplus\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{j}
that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}(\bigoplus\mathcal{U}_{j})
the port
p\in\mathcal{P}(\bigoplus\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{j})
, hence
(\varphi^{\prime}_{j})^{-1}\circ\varphi_{\oc(j)}\circ\varphi_{j}
is an isomorphism
B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i+1](e)}(\oc(j))\simeq B_{\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[i+1](e^{\prime})}(\oc^{\prime}(j))
.

A set of two well-chosen terms of the Taylor expansion are already enough to characterize a PS:

Theorem 50
.

For any PS
R
having
\mathcal{T}
as Taylor expansion, there exists a finite subset
\mathcal{T}_{0}
of
\mathcal{T}
with
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{T}_{0}\right)=2
such that, for any PS
R^{\prime}
having
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
as Taylor expansion, for any
\mathcal{T}_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
, we have
(\mathcal{T}_{0}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{0}^{\prime}\Rightarrow R\equiv R^{\prime})
.

Proof 3.25
.

From
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1})
for
e_{1}
a
1
-experiment, one can compute
\beta(R)
. Indeed, one can easily check by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
that we have:

•

\textit{co-size}(R)=\textit{co-size}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1}))

•

\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R)\right)=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1}))\right)

•

\Theta(R)=\Theta(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1}))

One can then take for
\mathcal{T}_{0}
the set
\{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1}),\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e)\}
, where
e
is any
k
-heterogeneous pseudo-experiment on
R
with
k\geq\beta(R)
. Indeed:

Let
R^{\prime}
be a PS having
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
as Taylor expansion and let
\{T^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime}_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
such that
T^{\prime}_{1}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e_{1})
and
T^{\prime}_{2}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](e)
. There exist a pseudo-experiment
e^{\prime}_{1}
on
R^{\prime}
such that
T^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](e^{\prime}_{1})
and a pseudo-experiment
e^{\prime}
on
R^{\prime}
such that
T^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](e^{\prime})
. By Corollary
25
, the pseudo-experiment
e^{\prime}
is a
k
-heterogeneous experiment on
R^{\prime}
. So we can apply Proposition
49
.

In particular, we obtain the invertibility of Taylor expansion:

Corollary 51
.

Let
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
be two PS’s having respectively
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
as Taylor expansions. If
\mathcal{T}_{1}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{2}
, then
R_{1}\equiv R_{2}
.

The finite subset
\mathcal{T}_{0}
of Theorem
50
has cardinality
2
. A natural question is to ask if the theorem could be refined in such a way that one could have a singleton
\mathcal{T}_{0}
(for any PS). The answer is: no, it is not possible.

Proposition 52
.

There exists a PS
R
having
\mathcal{T}
as Taylor expansion such that, for any
T\in\mathcal{T}
, there exist a PS
R^{\prime}
having
\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
as Taylor expansion and
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T^{\prime}}
such that
T\equiv T^{\prime}
holds but
R\equiv R^{\prime}
does not hold.

Proof 3.26
.

For
R
, we take the PS of depth
1
depicted in Figure
39
.

4.
Relational semantics

4.1.
Untyped framework

For the semantics of PS’s in the multiset based relational semantics, we are given a set
\mathcal{A}
that does not contain any couple nor any
3
-tuple and such that
\ast\notin\mathcal{A}
. We define, by induction on
n
, the sets
D_{\mathcal{A},n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

•

D_{\mathcal{A},0}=\{+,-\}\times(\mathcal{A}\cup\{\ast\})

•

D_{\mathcal{A},n+1}=D_{\mathcal{A},0}\cup(\{+,-\}\times D_{\mathcal{A},n}\times D_{\mathcal{A},n})\cup(\{+,-\}\times\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(D_{\mathcal{A},n}))

We set
D_{\mathcal{A}}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}D_{\mathcal{A},n}
.

•

For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we denote by
0pt{\alpha}
the integer
\min\{n\in\mathbb{N};\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A},n}\}
. For any finite set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we set
0pt{x}=\max\{0pt{x(p)};p\in\mathcal{P}\}
.

•

For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define the integer
\textit{size}(\alpha)
by induction on
0pt{\alpha}
: for any
\delta\in\{+,-\}
, we set
\textit{size}((\delta,\gamma))=1
if
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}\cup\{\ast\}
,
\textit{size}((\delta,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}))=1+\textit{size}(\alpha_{1})+\textit{size}(\alpha_{2})
if
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, and
\textit{size}((\delta,[\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}]))=1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\textit{size}(\alpha_{j})
. For any finite set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we set
\textit{size}(x)=\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\textit{size}(x(p))
.

•

For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we denote by
\textit{At}(\alpha)
the set of
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
that occur in
\alpha
. For any finite set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we set
\textit{At}(x)=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathsf{im}(x)}\textit{At}(\alpha)
.

{defi}

For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define
\alpha^{\perp}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
as follows:

•

if
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}\cup\{\ast\}
and
\delta\in\{+,-\}
, then
(\delta,\alpha)^{\perp}=(\delta^{\perp},\alpha)
;

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\alpha^{\perp}=(\delta^{\perp},{\alpha_{1}}^{\perp},{\alpha_{2}}^{\perp})
;

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,[\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}])
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\alpha^{\perp}=(\delta^{\perp},[{\alpha_{1}}^{\perp},\ldots,{\alpha_{m}}^{\perp}])
;

where
+^{\perp}=-
and
-^{\perp}=+
.

The following definition is an adaptation of the original definition in
[
19
]
to our framework. If
e
is an experiment on some PS
S
, then
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}
is its
result
. Then the interpretation of a PS is the set of results of its experiments:

{defi}

Let
S
be a differential in-PS. We define, by induction on
\textit{depth}(S)
, the set of
experiments on
S
: it is the set of pairs
e=(\mathcal{P}(e),\mathcal{B}(e))
, where

•

\mathcal{P}(e)
is a function that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(S)
an element of
D_{\mathcal{A}}
and with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(S)
an element of
\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(D_{\mathcal{A}})
,

•

and
\mathcal{B}(e)
is a function which associates with every
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
a finite multiset of experiments on
B_{S}(o)

such that

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{m}}(S)
, for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(S)
such that
t_{\mathcal{G}(S)}(w_{1})=p=t_{\mathcal{G}(S)}(w_{2})
,
w_{1}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{G}(S))
and
w_{2}\notin\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{G}(S))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=(\delta,\mathcal{P}(e)(w_{1}),\mathcal{P}(e)(w_{2}))
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
(l_{\mathcal{G}(S)}(p)=\otimes\Leftrightarrow\delta=+)
;

•

for any
\{p,p^{\prime}\}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(S)\cup\mathcal{C}_{0}(S)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)={\mathcal{P}(e)(p^{\prime})}^{\perp}
;

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(S)
,
we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=(\delta,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(S)\\
t_{\mathcal{G}(S)}(w)=p\end{subarray}}[\mathcal{P}(e)(w)]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(S)\\
t_{S}(q)=p\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}(e)(q))
and
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
(l_{\mathcal{G}(S)}(p)=\oc\Leftrightarrow\delta=+)
;

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{1}(S)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=(+,\ast)
and, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\bot}(S)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=(-,\ast)
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{S}(o))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}

\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot[\mathcal{P}(e_{o})(p)]
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(B_{S}(o))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}

\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot\mathcal{P}(e_{o})(p)
.

If
S
is a PS, then we set
\llbracket S\rrbracket=

\{\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}};

e\textit{ is an experiment on }S\}
.

Every experiment induces a pseudo-experiment:

{defi}

Let
S
be an in-PS. Let
e
be an experiment on
S
. Then we define, by induction on
\textit{depth}(S)
, a pseudo-experiment
\overline{e}
on
S
as follows:

•

\overline{e}(\varepsilon)=0

•

and, for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
,
\overline{e}(o)=\bigcup_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(e(o))}\{\overline{e_{o}}[\varepsilon\mapsto i];1\leq i\leq\mathcal{B}(e)(e_{o})\}
.

{defi}

Let
r\in\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(D_{\mathcal{A}})
. We say that
r
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective
if, for any
(\delta,\gamma)\in\{+,-\}\times{\mathcal{A}}
, there is at most one occurrence of
(\delta,\gamma)
in
r
.

For any set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we say that
x
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective
if
\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}[x(p)]
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective; moreover a subset
D_{0}
of
(D_{\mathcal{A}})^{\mathcal{P}}
is said to be
\mathcal{A}
-injective if, for any
x\in D_{0}
, the function
x
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective.

An experiment
e
on a differential PS
S
is said to be
injective
if
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective.

{defi}

Let
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
. For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define
\sigma\cdot\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
as follows:

•

if
\alpha\in{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot(+,\alpha)=\sigma(\alpha)
and
\sigma\cdot(-,\alpha)=\sigma(\alpha)^{\perp}
;

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,\ast)
for some
\delta\in\{+,-\}
, then
\sigma\cdot\alpha=\alpha
;

•

if
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot(\delta,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(\delta,\sigma\cdot\alpha_{1},\sigma\cdot\alpha_{2})
;

•

if
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot(\delta,[\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}])=(\delta,[\sigma\cdot\alpha_{1},\ldots,\sigma\cdot\alpha_{m}])
.

For any set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define a function
\sigma\cdot x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
by setting:
(\sigma\cdot x)(p)=\sigma\cdot x(p)
for any
p\in\mathcal{P}
.

Fact 53
.

For any
\sigma:A\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, for any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we have
\sigma\cdot{\alpha}^{\perp}={(\sigma\cdot\alpha)}^{\perp}
.

Proof 4.1
.

By induction on
\alpha
.

Lemma 54
.

For any functions
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, for any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we have
\sigma\cdot(\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\alpha)=(\sigma\cdot\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\alpha
.

Proof 4.2
.

By induction on
\alpha
, applying Fact
53
.

{defi}

Let
S
be a differential in-PS. Let
e
be an experiment on
S
. Let
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
. We define, by induction of
\textit{depth}(S)
, an experiment
\sigma\cdot e
on
S
by setting

•

\mathcal{P}(\sigma\cdot e)=\sigma\cdot\mathcal{P}(e)

•

\mathcal{B}(\sigma\cdot e)(o)=\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot[\sigma\cdot e_{o}]
for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
.

Since we deal with untyped proof-nets, we cannot assume that the proof-nets are
\eta
-expanded and that experiments label the axioms only by atoms. That is why we introduce the notion of
atomic experiment
:

{defi}

For any differential in-PS
S
, we define, by induction on
\textit{depth}(S)
, the set of
atomic experiments on
S
: it is the set of experiments
e
on
S
such that

•

for any
\{p,q\}\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{G}(S))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p),\mathcal{P}(e)(q)\in\{+,-\}\times{\mathcal{A}}
;

•

and, for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(S)
, the multiset
\mathcal{B}(e)(o)
is a multiset of atomic experiments on
B_{S}(o)
.

{defi}

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a set.

Let
x\in{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{P}}
. A
renaming of
x
is a function
\sigma:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
(\forall\gamma\in\textit{At}(x))\sigma(\gamma)\in\{+,-\}\times\mathcal{A}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\subseteq{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{P}}
.
Let
x\in\mathcal{D}
, we say that
x
is
\mathcal{D}
-atomic
if we have

(\forall\sigma\in{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{A}})(\forall y\in\mathcal{D})(\sigma\cdot y=x\Rightarrow\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(y))

We denote by
\mathcal{D}_{\textit{At}}
the subset of
\mathcal{D}
consisting of the
\mathcal{D}
-atomic elements of
\mathcal{D}
.

Fact 55
.

Let
x\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\sigma
and
\tau
be two applications
\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then we have
(\sigma\cdot\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)\Rightarrow(\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)\wedge\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(\tau\cdot x)))
.

Proof 4.3
.

Let us assume that
\sigma\cdot\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
. By Lemma
54
, we have
(\sigma\cdot\tau)\cdot x=\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot x)
. So, we have
\textit{size}(\tau\cdot x)\leq\textit{size}(\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot x))=\textit{size}((\sigma\cdot\tau)\cdot x)=\textit{size}(x)\leq\textit{size}(\tau\cdot x)\leq\textit{size}(\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot x))
, hence
\textit{size}(\tau\cdot x)=\textit{size}(x)
and
\textit{size}(\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot x))=\textit{size}(\tau\cdot x)
, which entails that
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(\tau\cdot x)
.

Fact 56
.

Let
S
and
S^{\prime}
be two cut-free differential PS’s of depth
0
. Let
e
and
e^{\prime}
be two atomic experiments on
S
and
S^{\prime}
respectively such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S)}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(S^{\prime})}}
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective. Then
S\equiv S^{\prime}
.

Proof 4.4
.

By induction on
\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}(S)\right)
.

For any PS
R
, any
\llbracket R\rrbracket
-atomic injective point is the result of some atomic experiment on
R
:

Fact 57
.

Let
R
be a cut-free in-PS. Let
x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
. We assume that the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite or
x
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective. Then there exists an atomic experiment
e
on
R
such that
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=x
.

Proof 4.5
.

We prove, by induction on
(\textit{depth}(R),\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)\right))
lexicographically ordered, that, for any non-atomic experiment
e^{\prime}
on
R
, there exist an experiment
e
on
R
, a function
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma\cdot e=e^{\prime}
and
\gamma\in\textit{At}(\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}})
such that
\sigma(\gamma)\notin\{+,-\}\times{\mathcal{A}}
.

The converse does not necessarily hold (but see Lemma
68
about
typable
cut-free PS’s): for some cut-free PS’s
R
, there are results of atomic injective experiments on
R
that are not
\llbracket R\rrbracket
-atomic. Indeed, consider Figure

.

There exists an atomic injective experiment

where

. But
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=6.45059pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=7.03615pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}

,
