\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})(p_{2})=(-,[(-,\gamma_{1}),\ldots,(-,\gamma_{8}),(-,(-,\gamma_{10}),(-,\gamma_{11})),\ldots,(-,(-,\gamma_{22}),(-,\gamma_{23}))])

•

and
\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})(p_{3})=(-,[(+,[(+,\ast),(+,\ast)]),(+,[(+,\ast),(+,\ast),(+,\ast)])])
;

we set
\sigma(\gamma)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(+,\gamma)&\textit{if $\gamma\in{\mathcal{A}}\setminus\{\gamma_{8}\}$;}\\
(+,(+,\gamma_{8}),(+,\gamma_{9}))&\textit{if $\gamma=\gamma_{8}$;}\end{array}\right.
- we have
\sigma\cdot\mathchoice{{e^{\prime}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e^{\prime}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e^{\prime}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=6.45059pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e^{\prime}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=7.03615pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}=\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=5.57225pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=6.45059pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=7.03615pt}}_{\,\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}}}
.

But it does not matter, because yet atomic points are enough to generate all the points:

Fact 58
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. For any
y\in\llbracket R\rrbracket
, there exist
x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
and
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma\cdot x=y
.

Proof 4.6
.

By induction on
\textit{size}(\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}[y(p)])
: if
y\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
, then we can set
x=y
and
\sigma=id_{\mathcal{A}}
; if
y\notin{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
, then there exist a function
\sigma^{\prime}:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
,
y^{\prime}\in\llbracket R\rrbracket
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\cdot y^{\prime}=y
and
\gamma\in\textit{At}(y^{\prime})
such that
\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma)\notin{\mathcal{A}}
, hence
\textit{size}(\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}[y^{\prime}(p)])<\textit{size}(\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}[y(p)])
. By induction hypothesis, there exist
x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\cdot x=y^{\prime}
. We set
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}
: we have
\sigma\cdot x=(\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime})\cdot x=\sigma^{\prime}\cdot(\sigma^{\prime\prime}\cdot x)=\sigma^{\prime}\cdot y^{\prime}=y
.

Lemma 59
.

Let
R
be an in-PS. Let
e
be an (resp. atomic) experiment on
R
. Then there exists an (resp. atomic) experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
on
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=4.21695pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.8025pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
.

Proof 4.7
.

We prove, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, that, for any in-PS
R
, for any experiment
e
on
R
, there exists an experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
on
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
such that

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))(p)
;

•

and, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(R)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))\\
\kappa_{R}[0](e)(q)=p\end{subarray}}[\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))(q)]
.

One can take for
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
the following experiment on
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
:

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
,
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))(p)=\mathcal{P}(e)(p)
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
, for any
e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[0](\overline{e_{o}}))
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))((o,(e_{o},p))=\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}(e_{o}))(p).

{defi}

Let
k>1
. An experiment
e
on some in-PS
R
is said to be
k
-heterogeneous
if the pseudo-experiment
\overline{e}
is
k
-heterogeneous.

{defi}

Let
k>1
.
For any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is any set, we say that
x
is
k
-heterogeneous
if the following properties hold:

•

for any multiset
a
, the pair
(+,a)
occurs at most once in
\mathsf{im}(x)
and, if it occurs, then there exists
j>0
such that the cardinality of
a
is
k^{j}
;

•

for any multisets
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
having the same cardinality such that the pairs
(+,a_{1})
and
(+,a_{2})
occur in
\mathsf{im}(x)
, we have
a_{1}=a_{2}
.

Lemma 60
.

Let
R
be a cut-free in-PS. Let
k>1
. For any experiment
e
on
R
, if
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
k
-heterogeneous, then
e
is
k
-heterogeneous. Conversely, if
e
is an atomic
k
-heterogeneous experiment on
R
, then
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
k
-heterogeneous.

Proof 4.8
.

Let
e
be an experiment on
R
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
k
-heterogeneous. By Lemma
59
, there exists an experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
on
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=4.21695pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.8025pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}=

\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. Since
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
is cut-free, we have:

(1)

{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}[\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))]\subseteq\{k^{j};j>0\}

(2)

(\forall p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})))({\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}(p_{1})={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}(p_{2})\Rightarrow p_{1}=p_{2})

By Corollary
25
, the pseudo-experiment
\overline{e}
is
k
-heterogeneous.

Conversely, let
e
be an atomic
k
-heterogeneous experiment on
R
. Then, by Lemma
59
, there exists an atomic experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
of
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=4.21695pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.8025pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. By Corollary
25
, we have:

(1)

{\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}[\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))]\subseteq\{k^{j};j>0\}

(2)

(\forall p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\oc}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})))({\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}(p_{1})={\textit{a}}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})}(p_{2})\Rightarrow p_{1}=p_{2})

Since
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
is atomic,
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=4.21695pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.8025pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}
is
k
-heterogeneous.

Lemma 61
.

Let
R
and
R^{\prime}
be two cut-free PS’s such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
. Let
e
be an injective atomic experiment on
R
and let
e^{\prime}
be an injective atomic experiment on
R^{\prime}
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}})
.

Proof 4.9
.

By Lemma
59
, there exists an atomic experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R}(e)
on
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e})
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=4.21695pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R}(e))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=4.8025pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e}))}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
and an atomic experiment
\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}})
such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}(e^{\prime}))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.1282pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}(e^{\prime}))\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.1282pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}(e^{\prime}))\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=4.90167pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}}))}}{{\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}(e^{\prime}))\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=5.48724pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e^{\prime}}))}}=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}
. We apply Fact
56
.

Theorem 62
.

Let
R
be a cut-free PS. If the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite, then there exists an
\mathcal{A}
-injective subset
D_{0}
of
{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
with
\mathsf{Card}\left(D_{0}\right)=2
such that, for any cut-free PS
R^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
, one has
(D_{0}\subseteq{\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\Rightarrow R\equiv R^{\prime})
.

Proof 4.10
.

Let
f
be an injective
1
-experiment on
R
(its existence is ensured by the assumption that the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite). By Fact
58
, there exists
x_{1}\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
and
\sigma_{1}:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma_{1}\cdot x_{1}=\mathchoice{{f\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{f\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{f\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{f\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
; the point
x_{1}
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective too. Let
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
be the greatest cardinality of the negative multisets that occur in
\mathsf{im}(x_{1})
. Let
k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
be the number of occurrences of positive multisets in
\mathsf{im}(x_{1})
. Let
k>\max{\{k_{1},k_{2}\}}
and let
e
be an injective
k
-heterogeneous experiment on
R
(its existence is ensured by the assumption that the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite). By Fact
58
, there exist
x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
and
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma\cdot x=\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. We can take
D_{0}=\{x_{1},x\}
. Indeed:

Since the point
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective, the point
x
is
\mathcal{A}
-injective too. By Lemma
60
,
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
is
k
-heterogeneous, hence
x
too. Let
R^{\prime}
be a cut-free PS such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
and
D_{0}\subseteq{\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
. By Fact
57
, there exist an atomic experiment
f_{0}
(resp.
f_{0}^{\prime}
) on
R
(resp. of
R^{\prime}
) such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=x_{1}
(resp.
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(f_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}=x_{1}
) and an atomic experiment
e_{0}
(resp.
e_{0}^{\prime}
) on
R
(resp. of
R^{\prime}
) such that
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=x
(resp.
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathcal{P}(e_{0}^{\prime})\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}=x
). By Lemma
61
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{R}[0](\overline{e_{0}})\equiv\mathcal{T}_{R^{\prime}}[0](\overline{e_{0}^{\prime}})
.

Moreover, the experiments
f_{0}
and
f_{0}^{\prime}
are
1
-experiments on
R
and
R^{\prime}
respectively (all the positive multisets of
x_{1}
have cardinality
1
). We have:

•

\textit{co-size}(R)=k_{1}=\textit{co-size}(R^{\prime})
;

•

\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R)\right)=k_{2}=\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(R^{\prime})\right)
;

•

and
\Theta(R)=0=\Theta(R)
(because
R
and
R^{\prime}
are cut-free).

We thus have
k\geq\max{\{\beta(R),\beta(R^{\prime})\}}
. Finally, by Lemma
60
, the experiments
e_{0}
and
e_{0}^{\prime}
are
k
-heterogeneous. We can then apply Proposition
49
to obtain
R\equiv R^{\prime}
.

Corollary 63
.

Let
R
and
R^{\prime}
be two cut-free PS’s such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
. If the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite, then one has
(R\equiv R^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}={\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}})
.

4.2.
Typed framework

We want to apply Theorem
62
to obtain a similar result for typed PS’s (Theorem
73
). For that, we need to relate
\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket
to
{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
; it is the role of Lemma
69
.

{defi}

We assume that we are given a set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
for each
X\in\mathcal{X}
. Then, for any
C\in\mathbb{T}
, we define, by induction on
C
, the set
\llbracket C\rrbracket
as follows:
\llbracket 1\rrbracket=\{\ast\}=\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
;
\llbracket(C_{1}\otimes C_{2})\rrbracket=\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\times\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket(C_{1}\invamp C_{2})\rrbracket
;
\llbracket\oc C\rrbracket=\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(\llbracket C\rrbracket)=\llbracket\wn C\rrbracket
.

Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a typed differential in-PS. We define, by induction on
\textit{depth}(R)
, the set of
experiments on
(R,\mathsf{T})
: it is the set of pairs
e=(\mathcal{P}(e),\mathcal{B}(e))
, where

•

\mathcal{P}(e)
is a function that associates with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
an element of
\llbracket\mathsf{T}(p)\rrbracket
and with every
p\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(R)
an element of
\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(\llbracket\mathsf{T}(p)\rrbracket)
,

•

and
\mathcal{B}(e)
is a function which associates with every
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
a finite multiset of experiments on
(B_{R}(o),\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}(B_{R}(o))}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=3.02223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}(B_{R}(o))}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=2.57834pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}(B_{R}(o))}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.1639pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}(B_{R}(o))}})

such that

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{m}}(R)
, for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(R)
such that
t_{\mathcal{G}(R)}(w_{1})=p=t_{\mathcal{G}(R)}(w_{2})
,
w_{1}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{G}(R))
and
w_{2}\notin\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{G}(R))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=(\mathcal{P}(e)(w_{1}),\mathcal{P}(e)(w_{2}))
;

•

for any
\{p,p^{\prime}\}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(R)\cup\mathcal{C}_{0}(R)
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\mathcal{P}(e)(p^{\prime})
;

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{e}}(R)
,
we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(R)\\
t_{\mathcal{G}(R)}(w)=p\end{subarray}}[\mathcal{P}(e)(w)]+\sum_{o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)}\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(B_{R}(o))\\
t_{R}(o,q)=p\end{subarray}}\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot\mathcal{P}(e_{o})(q)
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(B_{R}(o))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot[\mathcal{P}(e_{o})(p)]
;

•

for any
o\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(R)
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{>0}(B_{R}(o))
, we have
\mathcal{P}(e)(p)=\sum_{e_{o}\in\textit{Supp}(\mathcal{B}(e)(o))}\mathcal{B}(e)(o)(e_{o})\cdot\mathcal{P}(e_{o})(p)
.

For any experiment
e=((R,\mathsf{T}),\mathcal{P}(e),\mathcal{B}(e))
, we set
\mathcal{P}(e)=\mathcal{P}(e)
and
\mathcal{B}(e)=\mathcal{B}(e)
. We set
\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket=

\{\mathchoice{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathcal{P}(e)\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}};

e\textit{ is an experiment on }(R,\mathsf{T})\}
.

From now on, we assume that, for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
does not contain any couple nor any
3
-tuple and
\ast\notin\mathcal{A}
and we assume that
\mathcal{A}=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal{X}}\llbracket X\rrbracket
. We define, by induction on
n
, the sets
{\overline{D}}_{\mathcal{A},n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

•

\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},0}=\mathcal{A}\cup\{\ast\}

•

\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},n+1}=\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},0}\cup(\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},n}\times\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},n})\cup\mathcal{M}_{\textit{fin}}(\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},n})

We set
\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A},n}
. We define the function
U:D_{\mathcal{A}}\to\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
as follows:

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,\gamma)
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}\cup\{\ast\}
, then
U(\alpha)=\gamma
;

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in D
, then
U(\delta,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(U(\alpha_{1}),U(\alpha_{2}))
;

•

if
\alpha=(\delta,\alpha_{0})
with
\delta\in\{+,-\}
and
\alpha_{0}\in D
, then
U(\delta,\alpha_{0})=U(\alpha_{0})
.

{defi}

Let
\sigma:{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{A}
. For any
\alpha\in\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define
\sigma\cdot\alpha\in\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
as follows:

•

if
\alpha\in{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot\alpha=\sigma(\alpha)
;

•

if
\alpha=\ast
, then
\sigma\cdot\alpha=\alpha
;

•

if
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(\sigma\cdot\alpha_{1},\sigma\cdot\alpha_{2})
;

•

if
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\in\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\sigma\cdot[\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}]=[\sigma\cdot\alpha_{1},\ldots,\sigma\cdot\alpha_{m}]
.

For any set
\mathcal{P}
, for any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define a function
\sigma\cdot x:\mathcal{P}\to\overline{D}_{\mathcal{A}}
by setting:
(\sigma\cdot x)(p)=\sigma\cdot x(p)
for any
p\in\mathcal{P}
.

For any function
\sigma:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define the function
U(\sigma):\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}
as follows:

U(\sigma)(\gamma)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U(\sigma(\gamma))&\textit{if $\sigma(\gamma)\in\{+,-\}\times\mathcal{A}$;}\\
\gamma&\textit{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Fact 64
.

For any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
, for any
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(\alpha)
, we have
U(\sigma\cdot\alpha)=U(\sigma)\cdot U(\alpha)
.

Fact 65
.

Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a typed in-PS. Then, for any experiment
e
on
R
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
, we have
0pt{e(p)}\geq 0pt{\mathsf{T}(p)}
.

{defi}

Any
\alpha\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
is said to be
uniform
if, for any occurrence of any multiset
a
that occurs in
\alpha
, for any
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\textit{Supp}(a)
, we have
0pt{\beta}=0pt{\beta^{\prime}}
.

For any finite set
\mathcal{P}
, any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
is said to be
uniform
if, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}
,
x(p)
is uniform.

Fact 66
.

Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a typed in-PS. Then, for any atomic experiment
e
on
R
, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R)
,
e(p)
is uniform and we have
0pt{e(p)}=0pt{\mathsf{T}(p)}
.

Fact 67
.

Let
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in D_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\sigma:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\alpha^{\prime}
is uniform and
\sigma\cdot\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha
. We have
0pt{\alpha}>0pt{\alpha^{\prime}}
if, and only if,
\sigma\notin\mathfrak{R}(\alpha^{\prime})
.

Proof 4.11
.

By induction on
0pt{\alpha}
.

Lemma 68
.

We assume that, for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
is infinite. Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a cut-free typed PS. Then, for any atomic experiment
e
on
R
, we have
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
.

Proof 4.12
.

Let
e
be an atomic experiment on
R
. Let
x^{\prime}\in\llbracket R\rrbracket
and let
\sigma:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\sigma\cdot x^{\prime}=\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. By Fact
58
, there exist
x\in\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}
and
\tau:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\tau\cdot x=x^{\prime}
. By Fact
57
and Fact
66
, the point
x
is uniform and
(\forall p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R))0pt{x(p)}=0pt{\mathsf{T}(p)}=e(p)
.
By Lemma
54
, we have
(\sigma\cdot\tau)\cdot x=\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot x)=\sigma\cdot x^{\prime}=\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. By Fact
67
, we have
\sigma\cdot\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
, hence, by Fact
55
,
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(\tau\cdot x)=\mathfrak{R}(x^{\prime})
, which entails
\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}\in\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}
.

Lemma 69
.

Let us assume that, for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
is infinite. Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a cut-free typed PS. Then
\{U\circ x;x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\}=\bigcup_{x\in\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket}\{\sigma\cdot x;\sigma\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{A}}\}
.

Proof 4.13
.

By Lemma
68
, we have
\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket\subseteq\{U\circ x;x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\}
. Let
x\in\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket
.
Now, for any
x^{\prime}\in\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}
, for any
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(x^{\prime})
, we have
\sigma\cdot x^{\prime}\in\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}
; indeed, let
x\in\llbracket R\rrbracket
and
\tau:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\tau\cdot x=\sigma\cdot x^{\prime}
; by Fact
57
and Fact
66
, we have
0pt{x}=0pt{x^{\prime}}
, hence, by Fact
67
,
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
. So, by Fact
64
, we have
\bigcup_{x\in\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket}\{\sigma\cdot x;\sigma\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{A}}\}\subseteq\{U(\sigma)\cdot(U\circ x);(x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\wedge\sigma\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{A}})\}=\{U\circ(\sigma\cdot x);(x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\wedge\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(x))\}=\{U\circ x;x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}\}
.

Conversely, let
x\in{\llbracket R\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
. If
\mathcal{X}=\emptyset
, then
U\circ x\in\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket
. Otherwise, the set
\mathcal{A}
is infinite, hence there exist an injective atomic experiment
e
on
R
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
such that
x=\sigma\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. Let
\tau:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that:

•

for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\textit{ax}}(R)
, for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
, for any
\delta\in\{+,-\}
such that
e(p)=(\delta,\gamma)
, we have
U(\tau)(\gamma)\in\llbracket\mathsf{T}(p)\rrbracket
;

•

and, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\textit{ax}}(R)\cap\mathcal{P}_{>0}(R)
, for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
, for any
\delta\in\{+,-\}
such that
(\delta,\gamma)\in\textit{Supp}(e(p))
, we have
U(\tau)(\gamma)\in\llbracket\mathsf{T}(p)\rrbracket
.

We have
U(\tau)\cdot(U\circ\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}})\in\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket
. It is clear that there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{R}(\tau\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}})
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\cdot(\tau\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}})=\sigma\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}
. We have
U\circ x=U\circ(\sigma\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}})=U\circ(\sigma^{\prime}\cdot(\tau\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}))=U(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot(U\circ(\tau\cdot\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}))=U(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot(U(\tau)\cdot(U\circ\mathchoice{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=3.44444pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=2.41112pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{e\,\smash{\vrule height=1.72221pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}))
(by applying Fact
64
twice).

{defi}

Let
\mathcal{D}\subseteq{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{P}}
for some set
\mathcal{P}
. We say that
\mathcal{D}

reflects renamings
if the following property holds:
(\forall x\in\mathcal{D})(\forall y\in D_{\mathcal{A}})(\forall\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(y))(\sigma\cdot y=x\Rightarrow y\in\mathcal{D})

{defi}

For any finite set
\mathcal{P}
, any function
x:\mathcal{P}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
is said to be
balanced
if, for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
, there are as many occurrences of
(+,\gamma)
in
\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}[x(p)]
as occurrences of
(-,\gamma)
in
\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}[x(p)]
.

Fact 70
.

Let
S
be a differential in-PS. Then, for any
x\in\llbracket S\rrbracket
,
x
is balanced.

Proof 4.14
.

By induction on
(\textit{depth}(S),\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{B}(S)\right),\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}(S)\right),\mathsf{Card}\left(\mathcal{C}_{0}(S)\right))
lexicographically ordered.

Fact 71
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be some finite set and let
\mathsf{T}
be a function
\mathcal{P}\to\mathbb{T}
. Let
x_{1},x_{2}\in{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{P}}

\mathcal{A}
-injective and balanced such that
U\circ x_{1}=U\circ x_{2}
and, for any
p\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
U(x_{1}(p))\in\llbracket\mathsf{T}(p)\rrbracket
. Then there exists
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(x_{1})
such that
\sigma\cdot x_{1}=x_{2}
.

Proof 4.15
.

By induction on
\sum_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\textit{size}(\mathsf{T}(p))
.

Lemma 72
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be some finite set.
Let
\mathcal{D}\subseteq{(D_{\mathcal{A}})}^{\mathcal{P}}
. Let
x\in\mathcal{D}
and let
\sigma\in\mathfrak{R}(x)
such that
\sigma\cdot x\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\textit{At}}
. Then
x\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\textit{At}}
.

Proof 4.16
.

Let
y\in\mathcal{D}
and let
\tau:\mathcal{A}\to D_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
\tau\cdot y=x
. Then, by Lemma
54
, we have
(\sigma\cdot\tau)\cdot y=\sigma\cdot(\tau\cdot y)=\sigma\cdot x
. We have
\sigma\cdot\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(y)
, hence, by Fact
55
,
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}(y)
.

Theorem
73
is similar to Theorem
62
:

Theorem 73
.

Let
(R,\mathsf{T})
be a cut-free typed PS. If, for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
is infinite, then there exists
D_{0}\subseteq\llbracket(R,\mathsf{T})\rrbracket
with
\mathsf{Card}\left(D_{0}\right)=2
such that, for any cut-free typed PS
(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}})
with
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
and
\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}
, we have
(D_{0}\subseteq\llbracket(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}})\rrbracket\Rightarrow(R,\mathsf{T})\equiv(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}))
.

Proof 4.17
.

By Theorem
62
, there exists an
\mathcal{A}
-injective subset
D^{\prime}_{0}=\{y,z\}
of
\llbracket R\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}
with
\mathsf{Card}\left(D_{0}\right)=2
such that, for any cut-free PS
R^{\prime}
, we have
(D^{\prime}_{0}\subseteq\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\textit{At}}\Rightarrow R\equiv R^{\prime})
. We can take
D_{0}=\{U\circ x;x\in D^{\prime}_{0}\}
. Indeed:

Let
(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}})
be a typed PS such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})
,
\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}{{\mathsf{T}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R)}}=\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.41428pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=6.01427pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=4.63649pt,depth=3.19223pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}{{\mathsf{T^{\prime}}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.37779pt,depth=3.77779pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime})}}
and
D_{0}\subseteq\llbracket(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}})\rrbracket
.
By Lemma
69
, there exist
y^{\prime},z^{\prime}\in{\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
such that
U\circ y=U\circ y^{\prime}
and
U\circ z=U\circ z^{\prime}
. By Fact
70
, we can apply Fact
71
to obtain that there exist
\sigma_{y}\in\mathfrak{R}(y)
and
\sigma_{z}\in\mathfrak{R}(z)
such that
\sigma_{y}\cdot y=y^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{z}\cdot z=z^{\prime}
. By Lemma
72
, we thus have
D^{\prime}_{0}\subseteq{\llbracket R^{\prime}\rrbracket}_{\textit{At}}
, hence
R\equiv R^{\prime}
. By Fact
2
, we obtain
(R,\mathsf{T})\equiv(R^{\prime},\mathsf{T^{\prime}})
.

Now, we take advantage of the normalization property of the typed PS’s:

Corollary 74
.

Let
(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})
and
(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})
be two typed PS’s such that
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})
and
\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T}_{1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{1}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=3.40471pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{1}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.99026pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})}}=\mathchoice{{\mathsf{T}_{2}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{2}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.55557pt,depth=4.07626pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{2}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.88889pt,depth=3.40471pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})}}{{\mathsf{T}_{2}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.77777pt,depth=3.99026pt}}_{\,\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})}}
. If, for any
X\in\mathcal{X}
, the set
\llbracket X\rrbracket
is infinite, then we have
((R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\simeq_{\beta}(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})\Leftrightarrow\llbracket(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\rrbracket=\llbracket(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})\rrbracket)
.

Proof 4.18
.

Let us assume that
\llbracket(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\rrbracket=\llbracket(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})\rrbracket
. There exist two cut-free typed PS’s
(R^{\prime}_{1},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{1})
and
(R^{\prime}_{2},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{2})
such that
(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\simeq_{\beta}(R^{\prime}_{1},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{1})
and
(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})\simeq_{\beta}(R^{\prime}_{2},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{2})
. We have
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime}_{1})=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{1})=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R_{2})=\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(R^{\prime}_{2})
and
\llbracket(R^{\prime}_{1},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{1})\rrbracket=\llbracket(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\rrbracket=\llbracket(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})\rrbracket=\llbracket(R^{\prime}_{2},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{2})\rrbracket
. By Theorem
73
, we have
(R^{\prime}_{1},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{1})\equiv(R^{\prime}_{2},\mathsf{T^{\prime}}_{2})
, hence
(R_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1})\simeq_{\beta}(R_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2})
.

Conclusion

The aim of this work was to prove theorems that relate the syntax of the proof-nets with their relational semantics firstly and with their Taylor expansion secondly. But incidentally we proved an interesting intrinsic semantical result: We showed that the entire relational semantics of any normalizable proof-net can be rebuilt from two well-chosen points (and that it is impossible to strengthen this result by rebuilding any proof-net from only one well-chosen point). So, in some way, these two points together could be seen as a principal typing of intersection types for the given proof-net. With the algorithm we described, we can first rebuild the normal form of the proof-net from this “principal typing” and then compute the semantics. Now, some technology could probably be developed to compute the semantics directly from these points without rebuilding the syntactic object, like in the case of untyped lambda-calculus (expansion, substitution…).

5.
Appendix: Proof of Lemma
41

Proof 5.1
.

With Lemma
18
in mind, we can describe
\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)
:

•

\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))=\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cup\bigcup_{e_{1}\in e(o)}\{(o,(e_{1},p));p\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\}
;

•

l_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}l_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p)&\textit{if $p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))$;}\\
\wn&\textit{otherwise;}\end{array}\right.

•

\kappa_{R_{o}}[i](e)(p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\kappa_{R}[i](e)(p)&\textit{if $p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))$;}\\
(o,\varphi_{o}(q^{\prime}))&\textit{if $p=\varphi_{e_{1}}(q^{\prime})$ for some $e_{1}\in e(o)$, $q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}$;}\\
q&\textit{if $p=q$;}\end{array}\right.

•

\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))=\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cup\bigcup_{e_{1}\in e(o)}\{(o,(e_{1},p));p\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\}
;

•

t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(p)
is the following port of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(o,(e_{1},\varphi_{o}(t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p))))&\textit{if $p=(o,(e_{1},p^{\prime}))$ with $e_{1}\in e(o)$ and $t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p)\in\mathcal{Q}$;}\\
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(p)&\textit{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

•

\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))=\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
and
\mathcal{A}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))=\mathcal{A}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))

•

\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))=\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))

•

B_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}=B_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}

•

\mathsf{dom}(t_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)})=\mathsf{dom}(t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)})
and
t_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}(o^{\prime},p)=

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(o,(e_{1},\varphi_{o}(t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(o^{\prime},p)))&\begin{array}[]{l}\textit{if $t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(o^{\prime},p)\in\mathcal{Q}$ and $o^{\prime}=(o,(e_{1},o^{\prime\prime}))$}\\
\textit{for some $e_{1}\in e(o)$, $o^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{1}))$;}\end{array}\\
t_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}(o^{\prime},p)&\textit{otherwise;}\end{array}\right.

For any
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\cup\mathcal{Q}\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}]
such that
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{p,p^{\prime}\}\cap\mathcal{Q}\right)\leq 1
, we have
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}p^{\prime}
if, and only if, one of the following properties holds:

•

p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{Q}
and
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}p^{\prime}

•

\{p,p^{\prime}\}\subseteq\{(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime\prime})),\varphi_{e_{o}}(t_{R}{(o,p^{\prime\prime})})\}
for some
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)

•

\{p,p^{\prime}\}\cap\mathcal{Q}
is some singleton
\{p^{\prime\prime}\}
with
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}
and
\{p,p^{\prime}\}\subseteq\{p^{\prime\prime},\varphi_{e_{o}}(p^{\prime\prime})\}

1) Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\not=\emptyset
. We have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])=(\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q})\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)]
, hence, by Lemma
36
,
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)]\>\>\>(\ast)
. Again, by Lemma
36
, we have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}\>\>\>(\ast\ast)
.

Let
w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)
: We have
w\notin\mathcal{P}
(hence, by
(\ast\ast)
,
w\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
). Again by Lemma
36
, there exists
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))
such that
w=(o,(e_{o},p))
. We distinguish between two cases:

•

p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
: we have
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=t_{R}(o,p)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{Q}
, hence
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=(o,(e_{o},\varphi_{o}(t_{R}(o,p))))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
;

•

p\notin\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
: we have
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
.

We thus have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\}

Conversely, let
w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
such that
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
. By
(\ast)
, there exists
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{B_{R}(o)}[i](e_{o}))
such that
w=(o,(e_{o},p))
. We distinguish between two cases:

•

p\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
: we have
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=(o,(e_{o},\varphi_{o}(t_{R}(o,p))))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
, hence
(o,(e_{o},\varphi_{o}(t_{R}(o,p))))\in\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)]
; so,
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=t_{R}(o,p)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, which shows that
(o,(e_{o},p))\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)

•

p\notin\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
: we have
t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))=t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(o,(e_{o},p))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\setminus\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}]\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
.

We thus have

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)

Moreover,
\mathcal{W}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])=\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)
; we thus showed:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\}

So,
T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}}\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)
.

Since
T
is connected through ports not in
\mathcal{P}
,
T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]
is connected through ports not in
\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{P}\cap\mathsf{dom}(\varphi_{e_{o}})]\cup(\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathsf{dom}(\varphi_{e_{o}}))=\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
.

Finally,
\textit{co-size}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])=\textit{co-size}(T)
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))=\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\setminus(\mathcal{W}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])\cup\bigcup\mathcal{C}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]))=((\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\mathcal{Q})\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)])\setminus(\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)\cup\bigcup\mathcal{C}_{0}(T))=(\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\setminus\mathcal{Q})\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)]\subseteq(\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Q})\cup\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)]=\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
and
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[\varphi_{e_{o}}])
(hence
p\notin\mathcal{Q}
, so
\mathsf{Card}\left(\{p,p^{\prime}\}\cap\mathcal{Q}\right)\leq 1
): We distinguish between three cases:

•

p,p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\setminus\mathcal{Q}
and
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}p^{\prime}
: we have
p\in\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
;

•

\{p,p^{\prime}\}\subseteq\{(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime\prime})),\varphi_{e_{o}}(t_{R}{(o,p^{\prime\prime})})\}
for some
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{R}^{\mathsf{f}}(o)
: either
p=\varphi_{e_{o}}(t_{R}(o,p^{\prime}))
(hence
p\in\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}]\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}})
or
p=(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime\prime}))
. From now on, let us assume that
p=(o,(e_{o},p^{\prime\prime}))
. We have
t_{R}(o,p^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}
. If
t_{R}(o,p^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, then
p^{\prime}=\varphi_{e_{o}}(t_{R}(o,p^{\prime\prime}))\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)[\varphi_{e_{o}}]
, which contradicts
p^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)[\varphi_{e_{o}}]
; we thus have
t_{R}(o,p^{\prime\prime})\notin\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
, which entails
p\in\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
.

•

\{p,p^{\prime}\}\cap\mathcal{Q}
is some singleton
\{p^{\prime\prime}\}
with
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}
and

p=\varphi_{e_{o}}(p^{\prime\prime})\in\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{P}\cap\mathcal{Q}]\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
.

In the three cases, we have
p\in\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
.

So, we showed
T[\varphi_{e_{o}}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}})
.

2) Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}})
such that
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{o}\rangle)
. We have
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}])=(\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\setminus\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}])\cup\{q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q};\varphi_{e_{o}}(q^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}[\varphi_{e_{o}}[\mathcal{Q}]]=\mathcal{Q}
and

, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

Moreover, we have
\mathcal{W}_{0}(T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}])=\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)
. Hence

\mathcal{W}_{0}(T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}])=\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

Since
T
is connected modulo
\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
,
T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]
is connected modulo
{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}[\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}\cap\mathsf{dom}({\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1})]\cup(\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}\setminus\mathsf{dom}({\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}))=\mathcal{P}
.

Finally,
\textit{co-size}(T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}])=\textit{co-size}(T)
and

. Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}])
and
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}p^{\prime}
and

. We distinguish between three cases:

•

,

, hence, since

, hence

;

•

p\notin\mathcal{Q}
and
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}
: we have
p\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{G}(T))\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}
; but
\mathcal{P}_{e_{o}}\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\subseteq\mathcal{P}
.

In the three cases, we have
p\in\mathcal{P}
.

So, we showed
T[{\varphi_{e_{o}}}^{-1}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
.

3) Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
. Notice that
q\notin\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
; indeed, since

, we have
(q\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\Rightarrow q\in\mathcal{P})
, but
q\notin\mathcal{P}
.

Since
(\forall w\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))w\notin\bigcup_{e_{1}\in e(o)}\{(o,(e_{1},p));p\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\}
, we have:

•

\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)=\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)

•

and
(\forall w\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)=t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)
.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{0}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w\in(\mathcal{W}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(T))\setminus\mathcal{P};t_{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e))}(w)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\}

So,
T\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
.

Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
and
q^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{e_{1}\in e(o)}\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}q^{\prime}
: We have
p\in\mathcal{Q}
. Since from
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)
and
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R_{o}\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
we deduce
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, we obtain
p\in\mathcal{P}
. We thus have
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
.

We showed
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
.

4) Let
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
such that
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
.

Let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)
and
q^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{e_{1}\in e(o)}\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)
such that
p\coh_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}q^{\prime}
: We have
p\in\mathcal{Q}
. Since from
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{T}_{R}[i](e)
and
(\forall e_{1}\in e(o))\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{P}_{0}(R\langle o,i,e_{1}\rangle)=\emptyset
we deduce
\mathcal{P}_{0}(T)\cap\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, we obtain
p\in\mathcal{P}
. We thus have
T\trianglelefteq_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)
.

We showed
T\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{T}_{R_{o}}[i](e)}^{k}(\mathcal{P})
.
